{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}\right)\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\left(\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}\right)\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\left(\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\prod_{n}\left(V\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{n}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\prod_{n}\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{n}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textstyle\prod_{n}\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{n}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Note that the top row is exact because
\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}
is flat by Proposition
2.4.3
, and the bottom row is exact by the exactness of countable products (AB4*). By the five-lemma, the proposition reduces to the case that
V=\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}
. By an argument with idempotents as in the proof of Proposition
2.4.3
, this reduces to showing that for countable index sets
I
and
J
, the natural map

(2.4.10)

\displaystyle\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\left(\textstyle\prod_{n}\hat{\oplus}_{J}\underline{R}\right)\xrightarrow{}\textstyle\prod_{n}\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\hat{\oplus}_{J}\underline{R}\right)

is an isomorphism.
7
7

7

More precisely, we can assume
V_{m}\simeq\hat{\oplus}_{I_{m}}\underline{R}
and
W_{n}\simeq\hat{\oplus}_{J_{n}}\underline{R}
for countable index sets
I_{m}
and
J_{n}
, and without loss of generality, we may assume that
I_{m}=J_{m}=\mathbb{Z}
(since finite-free
\underline{R}
-modules are direct summands of
\hat{\oplus}_{\mathbb{Z}}\underline{R}
).
By the discussion in the last paragraph of the proof of
[
7
, Proposition A.57]
(they write
A
for what we call
\underline{R}
and
M_{A}
for our
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}
and we are taking
F=\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
) we can identify

(2.4.11)

\displaystyle\textstyle\prod_{m}\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}=\left(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\underline{R},

since
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}=\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\underline{R}
, see Proposition
2.2.7
. Thus we can rewrite the left hand side of (
2.4.10
) as

(2.4.12)

\displaystyle\left(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\left(\hat{\oplus}_{J}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\left(\textstyle\prod_{n}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\underline{R}.

By
[
7
, Corollary A.59]
, the proposition reduces to proving that the natural map

(2.4.13)

\displaystyle\left(\textstyle\prod_{m}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\left(\textstyle\prod_{n}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\to\textstyle\prod_{n,m}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}

is an isomorphism; this is explained at the end of
[
7
, Proposition A.66]
, see
[
7
, Equation A.22]
.
∎

2.4.9.

Let
V=\varprojlim_{m}V_{m}
and
W=\varprojlim_{n}W_{n}
be strongly countably Fréchet
R
-modules written as inverse limits as in Definition
2.4.2
. We have the following proposition.

Proposition 2.4.10
.

The natural map

(2.4.14)

\displaystyle V\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W\to\varprojlim_{m,n}\left(V_{m}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{n}\right)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We follow the proof of
[
7
, Corollary A.67]
. As in the proof of Proposition
2.4.3
, there is an exact sequence

(2.4.15)

\displaystyle 0\to W\to\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}\to\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}\to 0.

By the flatness proved in Proposition
2.4.3
, it remains exact after taking the tensor product with
V
, giving

(2.4.16)

\displaystyle 0\to V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W\to V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}\to V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\textstyle\prod_{n}W_{n}\to 0.

Applying Proposition
2.4.8
gives us the exact sequence

(2.4.17)

\displaystyle 0\to V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W\to\textstyle\prod_{n}\left(V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W_{n}\right)\to\textstyle\prod_{n}\left(V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W_{n}\right)\to 0,

which exhibits
V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W
as the projective limit
\varprojlim_{n}\left(V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W_{n}\right)
.
The same argument after tensoring
W_{n}
with the exact sequence

(2.4.18)

\displaystyle 0\to V\to\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}\to\textstyle\prod_{m}V_{m}\to 0.

shows that the natural map

(2.4.19)

\displaystyle V\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W_{n}\to\varprojlim_{m}(V_{m}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W_{n})

is an isomorphism, completing the proof.
∎

Corollary 2.4.11
.

For strongly countably Fréchet
\underline{R}
-modules
V
and
W
of compact type, the natural map

(2.4.20)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\underline{R}}(V,\underline{R})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\Hom_{\underline{R}}(W,\underline{R})\to\Hom_{\underline{R}}(V\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W,\underline{R})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Write
V=\varprojlim V_{n},W=\varprojlim_{m}V_{m}
as before and define
H_{n,m}=V_{n}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}W_{m}
. It follows from Lemma
2.4.5
, Lemma
2.2.6
that the transition maps
H_{n+1,m}(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}\to H_{n,m}(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
and
H_{n,m+1}(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}\to H_{n,m}(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
have dense image. Moreover, it is clear that each
H_{n,m}
is a direct summand of
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}
for some countable set
I
. If we combine this analysis with Lemma
2.3.3
, we see that
H=\varprojlim_{n,m}H_{n,m}
is strongly countably Fréchet of compact type.

It now follows from Lemma
2.4.6
and Proposition
2.4.10
that we may identify the map of the corollary with

(2.4.21)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{n,m}V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{m}^{\ast}\to\varinjlim_{n,m}H_{n,m}^{\ast}.

We first note that it follows from Lemma
2.3.1
-(2) and Proposition
2.2.9
that the transition maps in the direct system
\{V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{m}^{\ast}\}
are trace class. It follows as in the proof of Lemma
2.3.3
that the natural maps

(2.4.22)

\displaystyle V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{m}^{\ast}\to H_{n,m}^{\ast}

identify the target with the double dual of the source. Consider the commutative diagram

(2.4.23)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{m}^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V_{n+1}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{m+1}^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{n,m}^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{n+1,m+1}^{\ast},}

where we note that the bottom arrow is the double dual of the top arrow. It follows from Lemma
2.3.1
-(3) that the dashed arrow exists in a way that makes both triangles commute. This shows that the natural map

(2.4.24)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{n,m}V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}W_{m}^{\ast}\to\varinjlim_{n,m}H_{n,m}^{\ast}

is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 2.4.12
.

Let
V=\varprojlim_{m}V_{m}
be a strongly countably Fréchet
\underline{R}
-module of compact type written as an inverse limit as in Definition
2.4.2
. If
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
is a morphism of uniform Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, then the natural map

(2.4.25)

\displaystyle V^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\to(V\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B})^{\ast}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Using Proposition
2.4.8
and Lemma
2.4.5
we find that
V\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\xrightarrow{\sim}\varprojlim_{n}(V_{n}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B})
is a strongly countably Fréchet
\underline{B}
-module. By Lemma
2.4.6
we may moreover identify the map of the corollary with

(2.4.26)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{n}V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\to\varinjlim_{n}(V_{n}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B})^{\ast}.

It follows as in the proof of Lemma
2.3.3
that the natural maps

(2.4.27)

\displaystyle V_{n}^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\to(V_{n}\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B})^{\ast}.

identify the target with the double dual of the source. By Proposition
2.2.9
(and the fact that
V
is of compact type) the transition maps in the direct system
\{V_{n}^{\ast}\}
are trace class. The corollary now follows as in the proof of Corollary
2.4.11
from Lemma
2.3.1
-(3).
∎

3.
Sheaves on v-stacks

In this section we introduce a way to think about sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on nice analytic adic spaces over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. In fact, we develop a theory that works for arbitrary small v-stacks on the site of affinoid perfectoid spaces over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. In §
3.1
we collect preliminaries about small v-stacks and sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on them, and introduce the notion of (fiercely) v-complete affinoid analytic adic spaces. In §
3.2
, we introduce certain standard sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules and check that they (and their duals) take the expected values on v-complete affinoid analytic adic spaces. We also introduce a notion of (locally strongly countably) Fréchet sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules in this setting, and similarly prove that they take the expected values on v-complete affinoid analytic adic spaces. In §
3.3
we prove a criterion for checking that a sheaf “of geometric origin” is Fréchet, and also prove the Künneth formula for sheaves “of geometric origin”.

3.1.
Preliminaries

We write
{\mathrm{Perfd}}
for the opposite of the category of perfectoid Huber pairs
(A,A^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, equipped with the v-topology, see
[
46
, Definition 8.1]
. We will consider stacks and sheaves in the v-topology, with an important example being the representable sheaf
\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
for
(A,A^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
. A sheaf (or stack)
X
on
{\mathrm{Perfd}}
in the v-topology is called
small
(see
[
46
, Section 4]
) if there is an uncountable strong limit cardinal
\kappa
, an index set
I
of cardinality bounded by
\kappa
, a collection of
\kappa
-small perfectoid Huber pairs
(A_{i},A_{i}^{+})
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for
i\in I
, and a surjection

(3.1.1)

\displaystyle\coprod_{i\in I}\operatorname{Spd}(A_{i},A_{i}^{+})\to X.

An important example of a small v-sheaf is
X^{\lozenge}
, where
X
is an analytic adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, see
[
46
, Definition 15.5]
. In the affinoid case, we will write
\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})^{\lozenge}
.

For a small v-stack we will write
X_{\mathrm{v}}
for the full subcategory of (small) v-sheaves
Y\to X
where
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
for
(A,A^{+})
a perfectoid Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. This definition is different from
[
46
, Definition 14.1]
, which takes the category of all (small) v-sheaves
Y\to X
. However, this does not affect the category of v-sheaves on
X_{v}
since every small v-sheaf
Y\to X
can be covered by a disjoint union of
\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
’s.

3.1.1.

Let us write
\operatorname{Prof}
for the category of profinite sets as before. There is a natural functor from
\operatorname{Prof}
to small sheaves on
{\mathrm{Perfd}}
sending
T\to\underline{T}=\operatorname{Spd}(C^{0}(T,\mathbb{Q}_{p}),C^{0}(T,\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
, where for a topological ring
Y
the ring
C^{0}(T,Y)
is the ring of continuous functions from
T
to
Y
, equipped with the compact-open topology. We note that by Lemma
2.1.9
, if
(R,R^{+})
is a Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, then so is
(C^{0}(T,R),C^{0}(T,R^{+}))
. If
(R,R^{+})
is moreover affinoid perfectoid, then so is
(C^{0}(T,R),C^{0}(T,R^{+}))
, see
[
46
, Example 11.12]
. We note that
\underline{T}
represents the functor sending
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
to the set of continuous functions from
|\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})|
to
T
; we will often use this in the case of
T=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

A sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X_{\mathrm{v}}
determines a sheaf of condensed sets
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{cond}}
on
X_{\mathrm{v}}
which takes
(A,A^{+})\in X_{\mathrm{v}}
to the condensed set
8
8

8

That this is a condensed set follows from the fact that if
S\to T
is a surjective map of profinite sets, then
\operatorname{Spd}(C^{0}(S,A),C^{0}(S,A^{+}))\to\operatorname{Spd}(C^{0}(T,A),C^{0}(T,A^{+}))
is a v-cover.
sending
T\in\operatorname{Prof}
to the set
\mathcal{F}((C^{0}(T,A),C^{0}(T,A^{+}))
. We will sometimes write
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{cond}}(-)=H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(-,\mathcal{F})
, and we note that the functor sending
(A,A^{+})\in X_{\mathrm{v}}
to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{cond}}(A,A^{+})
commutes with limits and filtered colimits.

3.1.2.

There is a presheaf of rings
\mathcal{O}
on
{\mathrm{Perfd}}
sending
(A,A^{+})\mapsto A
and similarly a presheaf
\mathcal{O}^{+}
of rings sending
(A,A^{+})\mapsto A^{+}
; these are sheaves by
[
46
, Theorem 8.7]
. Note that
\mathcal{O}^{+}[\frac{1}{p}]=\mathcal{O}
. If
X
is a small v-stack, then we will write
\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}
for the inverse image of
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{O}^{+}
along the structure map
X\to\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The following observation will be useful.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of small v-sheaves, then
f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}
and
f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from the definitions, since we are pulling back to slice categories. More precisely, for
\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to X
we have that
\left(f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\right)(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to X)
will be given by
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to X\to Y)=A
which equals
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to X)
.
∎

From now on we will write
f^{\ast}
for
f^{-1}
. Because of Lemma
3.1.3
, this will cause no ambiguity.

Corollary 3.1.4
.

Given a Cartesian diagram of small v-sheaves

(3.1.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

there is a natural base change isomorphism

(3.1.3)

\displaystyle g^{\ast}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to f^{\prime}_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}.

Proof.

There is a natural base change map
g^{\ast}f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to f^{\prime}_{\ast}(g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}
for abstract reasons, and we can identify
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}=(g^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}
using Lemma
3.1.3
. The natural base change map is moreover an isomorphism because base change always holds when restricting to slice categories, cf.
[
46
, Proposition 17.6]
.
∎

3.1.5.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a uniform Huber pair over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and consider
U=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
. Then we can consider
U^{\lozenge}=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
as a small v-sheaf on
{\mathrm{Perfd}}
as above. We can thus consider the condensed ring
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U^{\lozenge},\mathcal{O})
. Recall that
(R,R^{+})
as above is called v-complete, see
[
21
, Definition 9.6]
, if the natural map
(R,R^{+})\to(H^{0}(U^{\lozenge},\mathcal{O}),H^{0}(U^{\lozenge},\mathcal{O}^{+}))
is a bijection. The following lemma is implicit in
[
21
, Section 9]
.

Lemma 3.1.6
.

If
(R,R^{+})
is v-complete, then the condensed ring
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U^{\lozenge},\mathcal{O})
is isomorphic to the condensed ring
\underline{R}
associated to the topological ring
R
.

3.1.7.

Let
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
and
(R,R^{+})\to(Q,Q^{+})
be morphisms of uniform Huber pairs over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Recall that the fiber product
\operatorname{Spd}(P,P^{+})\times_{\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})}\operatorname{Spd}(Q,Q^{+})
is given by
\operatorname{Spd}(E,E^{+})
, where

(3.1.4)

\displaystyle E=\left(P^{+}\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{+}}Q^{+}\right)[\tfrac{1}{p}]

and where
E^{+}
is the integral closure of the image of
P^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{R^{+}}Q^{+}
in
E
. In this generality, we caution that
(E,E^{+})
may not have nice properties such as uniformity or
v
-completeness, even if all three terms in the fiber product have these properties. However, it follows from the construction and Proposition
2.1.11
and its proof that, for a profinite set
S
and a uniform Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we have

(3.1.5)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\underline{S}=\operatorname{Spd}(C^{0}(S,R),C^{0}(S,R^{+})).

Proof of Lemma
3.1.6
.

It follows from
[
34
, Lemma 3.6.26]
that we can find a faithfully profinite étale Galois morphism
(R,R^{+})\to(A,A^{+})
with
(A,A^{+})
affinoid perfectoid (note that v-complete implies uniform). Let
\Gamma
be the Galois group of
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\to\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
.

The condensed ring
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U^{\lozenge},\mathcal{O})
is naturally identified with
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O})^{\underline{\Gamma}}
using v-descent. On the other hand since
R
is v-complete we deduce from
[
21
, Lemma 9.7]
that the natural map
R\to A^{\Gamma}=H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O})^{\underline{\Gamma}}(\ast)
is a bijection. Since
R
has the subspace topology inside
A
, this means that
R=A^{\Gamma}
as topological rings. Since the functor
R\mapsto\underline{R}
is a right adjoint, it commutes with limits such as taking fixed points. It thus suffices to show that
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O})=\underline{A}
for all
(A,A^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
.

By definition, this means that we need to show that for
T\in\operatorname{Prof}
we have a natural identification
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O})(T)=C^{0}(T,A)
. But this is a tautology since

(3.1.6)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O})(T)

\displaystyle=H^{0}((\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\times\underline{T}),\mathcal{O})

(3.1.7)

\displaystyle=H^{0}(\operatorname{Spd}(C^{0}(T,A),C^{0}(T,A^{+})),\mathcal{O})

(3.1.8)

\displaystyle=C^{0}(T,A)

by equation (
3.1.5
) (and using that
C^{0}(T,A)
is perfectoid).
∎

3.1.8.

The class of v-complete Huber pairs over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is not known to have good permanence properties, for example under rational localisations, finite étale maps, or the formation of Tate-algebras. For our purposes, we need a class of v-complete Huber pairs that does have these permanence properties.

Definition 3.1.9
.

Consider the largest full subcategory
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
of the category of all v-complete Huber pairs
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
such that:

•

If
(R,R^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
, then for all rational localisations
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
, we have that
(B,B^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
.

•

If
(R,R^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
, then for all finite étale morphisms
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
, we have that
(B,B^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
.

•

If
(R,R^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
, then
(R\langle T\rangle,\overline{R^{+}\langle T\rangle})\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
.

Objects of
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{frc}}
are called
fiercely v-complete Huber pairs
.

Lemma 3.1.10
.

The following classes of Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
are fiercely v-complete:

•

Affinoid perfectoid Huber pairs.

•

Diamantine Huber pairs in the sense of
[
21
, Definition 11.1]
.

•

Huber pairs
(R,R^{+})
over
(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
with
K
a nonarchimedean field, such that
R
is a seminormal affinoid algebra over
K
.
9
9

9

Recall that an affinoid algebra over
K
is a quotient of
K\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{n}\rangle
for some
n
.

Proof.

Affinoid perfectoid Huber pairs are diamantine by
[
21
, Proposition 11.3]
, so the second bullet point implies the first. The second bullet point follows from the fact that diamantine Huber pairs are themselves stable under rational localisations (
[
21
, Theorem 11.10]
), finite étale morphisms (
[
21
, Lemma 11.13]
), and forming
Tate algebras (
[
21
, Corollary 11.5]
), and that diamantine Huber pairs are v-complete by definition.

To establish the third bullet point, we note that the affinoid algebras
(R,R^{+})
over
K
with
R
seminormal are v-complete (e.g. by
[
21
, Theorem 10.3]
). Moreover, being seminormal is preserved under rational localization and finite étale morphisms (
[
21
, Lemma 6.4]
), and under the formation of Tate algebras by
[
21
, Theorem 10.3, Lemma 11.4]
.
∎

Definition 3.1.11
.

An analytic adic space
Y
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is said to be
locally fiercely v-complete
if
Y
is locally of the form
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
with
(R,R^{+})
fiercely v-complete.

Lemma 3.1.12
.

Let
X,Y
be locally fiercely v-complete analytic adic spaces over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then the natural map

(3.1.9)

\displaystyle\hom(X,Y)\to\hom_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X^{\lozenge},Y^{\lozenge})

is a bijection.

Proof.

It follows from Definition
3.1.9
that
X
and
Y
are stably v-complete in the sense of
[
36
, Definition 9.5]
. The lemma is now a consequence of
[
36
, Lemma 9.8]
.
∎

Definition 3.1.13
.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a uniform Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We say that
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
is
strongly smooth
if
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
factors as

(3.1.10)

\displaystyle(R,R^{+})\to(R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle,\overline{R^{+}\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle})\xrightarrow{f}(P,P^{+}),

with
f
finite étale.

Lemma 3.1.14
.

Let
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
be a morphism of Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which
is strongly smooth. If
(R,R^{+})
is fiercely v-complete, then so is
(B,B^{+})
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Definition
3.1.9
and Definition
3.1.13
.
∎

3.1.15.

For any small v-stack
X
on
{\mathrm{Perfd}}
recall that we write
X_{\mathrm{v}}
for the category of
(A,A^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
together with a map
\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to X
, equipped with the v-topology. We will work in the category
\operatorname{Ab}(X_{\mathrm{v}})
of (small) sheaves of abelian groups on
X_{\mathrm{v}}
; this is a Grothendieck abelian category closed under small limits and colimits, see
[
48
, 03CO]
. We note that by definition, every cover in
X_{\mathrm{v}}
has a finite subcover; in other words, the objects
\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
are qcqs in the topos-theoretic sense. This has the consequence that filtered colimits of sheaves can be computed pointwise, see
[
48
, Tag 0738]
.

3.1.16.

For a small v-stack
X
we can now consider the abelian category of (sheaves of)
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules in
X_{\mathrm{v}}
, see
[
48
, Tag 03DA]
. By
[
48
, Tag 03DB]
, the forgetful functor from
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules to sheaves of abelian groups commutes with limits and colimits. As before every sheaf
F
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules can be considered as a sheaf of condensed
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules over the sheaf of condensed rings
\mathcal{O}_{X,\mathrm{cond}}
. In other words, for
(A,A^{+})\in X_{\mathrm{v}}
the condensed abelian group
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F)
is a condensed
\underline{A}
-module.

3.2.
Standard sheaves and Fréchet sheaves

For
I
an index set we consider the following sheaves of
\mathcal{O}^{+}
or
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
{\mathrm{Perfd}}

(3.2.1)

\displaystyle\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\varprojlim_{n}\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}/p^{n}

(3.2.2)

\displaystyle\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}

\displaystyle:=\left(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}\right)[\tfrac{1}{p}]

(3.2.3)

\displaystyle\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}

\displaystyle,\qquad(\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}].

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 3.2.1
.

For
(R,R^{+})
a v-complete Huber pair and
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
, there are natural isomorphisms

(3.2.4)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})

\displaystyle\simeq\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R^{+}}

(3.2.5)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\simeq\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}

(3.2.6)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})

\displaystyle\simeq\textstyle\prod_{I}\underline{R^{+}}

(3.2.7)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,(\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}])

\displaystyle\simeq(\textstyle\prod_{I}\underline{R^{+}})[\tfrac{1}{p}].

Proof.

The natural isomorphism
\underline{R^{+}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})
of Lemma
3.1.6
induces the third natural isomorphism upon taking the product, since products commute with global sections. The fourth isomorphism is induced from the third by taking a filtered colimit (which also commutes with global sections). The second isomorphism is similarly induced from the first, which we now explain how to construct.

For
n>0
, by taking the long exact sequence in condensed cohomology for
0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{+}\xrightarrow{p^{n}}\mathcal{O}^{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{+}/p^{n}\rightarrow 0
, we obtain an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\underline{R^{+}/p^{n}}\rightarrow H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+}/p^{n})\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}]\rightarrow 0.

By varying
n
and using that the first system is Mittag–Leffler, taking the inverse limit gives an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\underline{R^{+}}\rightarrow H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}]\rightarrow 0.

The first arrow is an isomorphism by assumption, so
\varprojlim_{n}H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}]=0
. Next we consider the long exact sequence in cohomology for

(3.2.8)

\displaystyle 0\to\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}\xrightarrow{p^{n}}\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}\to\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}/p^{n}\to 0,

giving

0\rightarrow\oplus_{I}\underline{R^{+}/p^{n}}\rightarrow H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}/p^{n})\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}]\rightarrow 0.

Taking the inverse limit and using the fact that the first system is Mittag–Leffler, we get an exact sequence

(3.2.9)

\displaystyle 0\to\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R^{+}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})\to\varprojlim_{n}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}])\to 0.

But now we observe that (using the fact that cohomology commutes with filtered colimits and thus with direct sums in a coherent site)

(3.2.10)

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\oplus_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}])

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\oplus_{I}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}])

(3.2.11)

\displaystyle\subset\varprojlim_{n}\textstyle\prod_{I}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}])

(3.2.12)

\displaystyle\subset\textstyle\prod_{I}\varprojlim_{n}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+})[p^{n}])=0

to conclude.
∎

We let
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{+}}
denote the internal hom in sheaves of
\mathcal{O}^{+}
-modules, and we let
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}
denote the internal hom in sheaves of
\mathcal{O}
-modules. The following result should be compared to Proposition
2.2.9
.

Proposition 3.2.2
.

The natural maps

(3.2.13)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{+}}\left(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+},\mathcal{O}^{+}\right)

\displaystyle\to\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}

(3.2.14)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}\right)

\displaystyle\to(\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}]

are isomorphisms. If
I
is countable, then the natural maps

(3.2.15)

\displaystyle\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{+}}\left(\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+},\mathcal{O}^{+}\right)

(3.2.16)

\displaystyle\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}\left((\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}],\mathcal{O}\right)

are isomorphisms.

Our proof of the proposition is very similar to our proof of Proposition
2.2.9
. Let
(R,R^{+})
denote a Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and let
\mathcal{O}^{?}
denote either
\mathcal{O}^{+}
or
\mathcal{O}
(we will use similar notation for Huber pairs later) and let
F
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}^{?}
-modules on
\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
. There is an evaluation map

(3.2.17)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{?}}(F,\mathcal{O}^{?}))\to\Hom_{\underline{R^{?}}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F),\underline{R^{?}}).

Lemma 3.2.3
.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and let
F
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}^{?}
-modules on
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
. If for all
(R,R^{+})\to(A,A^{+})\in\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})_{\mathrm{v}}
the natural map

(3.2.18)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R^{?}}}\underline{A^{?}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F)

is an isomorphism, then the evaluation map (
3.2.17
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We follow the proof of Lemma
2.1.8
: Fix a profinite set
S
, write
\underline{S}_{(R,R^{+})}
for
\underline{S}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
and consider the evaluation of (
3.2.17
) on
S

(3.2.19)

H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{?}}(F,\mathcal{O}^{?}))(S)\\
\to\Hom_{\underline{R^{?}}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F),\underline{R^{?}})(S).

We can identify the left hand side with the set

(3.2.20)

\displaystyle H^{0}(\operatorname{Spd}(C^{0}(S,R),C^{0}(S,R^{+})),\Hom_{\mathcal{O}^{?}}(F,\mathcal{O}^{?})),

so a section gives a map
f\in\hom_{\mathcal{O}^{?}}(F,\mathcal{O}^{?})
of sheaves on
(\underline{S}_{(R,R^{+})})_{\mathrm{v}}
. The map
f
is uniquely determined by the induced map of condensed sets (notation as in Lemma
2.1.8
)

(3.2.21)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\underline{S}_{(R,R^{+})},F)\to\underline{R^{?}_{S}}

since for any
\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to\underline{S}_{(R,R^{+})}
, there is a unique induced map

(3.2.22)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F)\to\underline{A^{?}}

given by
f\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}^{?}(\underline{S}_{(R,R^{+})})}\underline{A^{?}}
; this uses the assumption of the lemma.

The right hand side of (
3.2.19
) is the set of
\pi^{-1}\underline{R^{?}}
-linear homomorphisms (notation as in the proof of Lemma
2.1.8
)

(3.2.23)

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F)\to\pi^{-1}\underline{R^{?}}

on the site of profinite sets over
S
. Using the adjunction argument as in Lemma
2.1.8
, we identify this with the set of
\underline{R^{?}}
-linear homomorphisms

(3.2.24)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F)\to\underline{R^{?}_{S}},

which is identified with the left hand side of (
3.2.19
) as above.
∎

Corollary 3.2.4
.

For a v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the natural condensed evaluation maps

(3.2.25)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}^{+}}}(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+},\mathcal{O}^{+}))

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\underline{R}^{+}}(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R^{+}},\underline{R^{+}})

(3.2.26)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}}(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O},\mathcal{O}))

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\underline{R}}(\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R},\underline{R})

are isomorphisms. If
I
is moreover countable, then the natural condensed evaluation maps

(3.2.27)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}^{+}}}(\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+},\mathcal{O}^{+}))

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\underline{R}^{+}}(\textstyle\prod_{I}\underline{R^{+}},\underline{R^{+}})

(3.2.28)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}}}((\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}],\mathcal{O}))

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\underline{R}}((\textstyle\prod_{I}\underline{R})[\tfrac{1}{p}],\underline{R})

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

The first two statements are a direct consequence of Lemma
3.2.3
and the fact that the formation of
\hat{\oplus}_{I}R^{+}
and
\hat{\oplus}_{I}R
commute with the solid tensor product, see Proposition
2.2.7
. For the third and fourth claim, we slightly modify the argument of Lemma
3.2.3
. Write

\displaystyle F^{?}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+}&\mbox{if }?=+\\
(\textstyle\prod_{I}\mathcal{O}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}]&\mbox{if }?=\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle F^{?}_{R}

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\textstyle\prod_{I}\underline{R^{+}}&\mbox{if }?=+\\
(\textstyle\prod_{I}\underline{R^{+}})[\tfrac{1}{p}]&\mbox{if }?=\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

It follows from Proposition
2.2.9
, Proposition
2.2.11
and Lemma
3.2.1
that for all morphisms
(A,A^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
of v-complete Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the natural map

(3.2.33)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F^{?})\otimes_{\underline{A^{?}}}\underline{B^{?}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+}),F^{?})

identifies the target with the double dual of the source. Using this, we can write down an explicit inverse of the natural map of the corollary: For a fixed profinite set
S
we can identify the natural map as in the proof of Lemma
3.2.3
with

(3.2.34)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),\Hom_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}^{?}}}(F^{?},\mathcal{O}^{?}))(S)=H^{0}(\operatorname{Spd}(R_{S},R^{+}_{S}),\Hom_{\mathcal{\mathcal{O}^{?}}}(F^{?},\mathcal{O}^{+}))\to

(3.2.35)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\underline{R}^{?}}(F^{?}_{R},\underline{R^{?}})(S)=\hom_{\underline{R}^{?}}(F^{?}_{R},\underline{R^{?}_{S}}).

An inverse is now given by taking
f:F^{?}_{R}\to\underline{R^{?}_{S}}
to the morphism of sheaves over
\operatorname{Spd}(R_{S},R_{S}^{+})
whose evaluation on
(R_{S},R_{S}^{+})\to(A,A^{+})
is: We first extend
f
to a morphism
F^{?}_{R_{S}}\to\underline{R^{?}_{S}}
by taking (solid) extension of scalars along
\underline{R^{?}}\to\underline{R_{S}^{?}}
and then taking double dual, we then take solid extension of scalars of
f
along
\underline{R^{?}_{S}}\to\underline{A^{?}}
and take double dual again.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.2.2
.

All statements are a direct consequence of Proposition
2.2.9
and Corollary
3.2.4
.
∎

3.2.5.

Let
Y
be a small v-sheaf and let
F
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
Y_{\mathrm{v}}
. We say that
F
is
countably orthonormalizable Banach
if
F\simeq\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
for some countable set
I
. We say that
F
is
locally countably orthonormalizable Banach
if there is a v-cover
Y^{\prime}\to Y
such that the pullback of
F
to
Y^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}
for some countable set
I
. We say that
F
is
reflexive
if the natural map
F\rightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}}(F,\mathcal{O}))
is an isomorphism.

Corollary 3.2.6
.

If
F
is a locally countably orthonormalizable Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}
-modules on
Y_{\mathrm{v}}
, then the sheaf
F
is reflexive.

Proof.

This can be checked locally in the topology, where it follows from Proposition
3.2.2
.
∎

We need the following refinement.

Corollary 3.2.7
.

Suppose that
F
is v-locally a direct summand of a locally countably orthonormalizable Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules. Then
F
is reflexive.

Proof.

The statement is local, so we may suppose that
F=F_{1}\xrightarrow{\oplus}F_{2}
where
F_{2}
is a locally free Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules; let
\Theta
be an idempotent of
F_{2}
with kernel
F_{1}
. The point is now that splitting an idempotent is both a limit and a colimit, hence using Corollary
3.2.6
we get

(3.2.36)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F_{1},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F_{2},\mathcal{O}_{Y})^{\Theta=0},\mathcal{O}_{Y})

(3.2.37)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F_{2},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})^{\Theta=0}

(3.2.38)

\displaystyle=F_{1}.

∎

Definition 3.2.8
.

A sheaf
F
of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules is called
locally strongly countably Fréchet
if there is a v-cover
Y^{\prime}=\coprod_{j\in J}\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\to Y
with
(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
such that the pullback of
f_{j}^{\ast}F
to
\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})
can be written as an inverse limit
f_{j}^{\ast}F=\varprojlim_{n}F_{n}
with the property that: T he sheaf
F_{n}
is isomorphic to a direct summand of
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}
for some countable set
I
and the transition maps
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+}),F_{n+1})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+}),F_{n})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
have dense image. If the transition maps
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+}),F_{n+1})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+}),F_{n})
are moreover compact, then
F
is called
locally strongly countably Fréchet of compact type
.

Lemma 3.2.9
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
with
(R,R^{+})
a v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and let
F=\varprojlim_{n}F_{n}
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules such that for all
n
the sheaf
F_{n}
is isomorphic to a direct summand of
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
for some countable set
I
, and such that the transition maps
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F_{n+1})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F_{n})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
have dense image. Then for
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
a morphism of v-complete Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the natural map

(3.2.39)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{B}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+}),F)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The formation of
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\underline{R}
commutes with the solid tensor product (Proposition
2.2.7
), so the lemma holds for
F_{n}
for all
n
since tensor products and global sections commute with idempotent projections. It follows from the definitions that
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F)
is a strongly countably Fréchet
\underline{R}
-module, and thus it follows from Proposition
2.4.10
that

(3.2.40)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{B}

\displaystyle=\left(\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F_{n})\right)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{B}

(3.2.41)

\displaystyle\simeq\varprojlim_{n}\left(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F_{n})\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{B}\right).

Since the lemma holds for each
F_{n}
, we can identify this with

(3.2.42)

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}\left(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F_{n})\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{B}\right)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\left(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+}),F_{n})\right)

(3.2.43)

\displaystyle=H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+}),F).

∎

Lemma 3.2.10
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
with
(R,R^{+})
a v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and let
F=\varprojlim_{n}F_{n}
be a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules such that for all
n
the sheaf
F_{n}
is isomorphic to a direct summand of
\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
for some countable set
I
, and such that the transition maps
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F_{n+1})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+}),F_{n})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
have dense image. Then the natural map

(3.2.44)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{n}\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F_{n},\mathcal{O}_{Y})\to\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\varprojlim_{n}F_{n},\mathcal{O}_{Y})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.2.9
, the assumptions of Lemma
3.2.3
hold. The result then follows from Lemma
3.2.3
and Lemma
2.4.6
.
∎

The statement of Lemma
3.2.10
can be thought of as saying that
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
is the dual “equipped with the weak topology”. Indeed, the filtered colimit of the weak topologies does indeed give the weak topology on the dual of the inverse limit (given that every functional factors through some stage of the inverse limit).

Corollary 3.2.11
.

A locally strongly countably Fréchet
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module
F
is reflexive.

Proof.

This can be proved locally in the v-topology, so we may assume that we are in the setting of Lemma
3.2.10
. Then it follows from Lemma
3.2.10
that

(3.2.45)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\varprojlim_{n}F_{n},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\varinjlim_{n}\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F_{n},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

(3.2.46)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(F_{n},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

(3.2.47)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}F_{n},

where we used Corollary
3.2.7
in the last step.
∎

3.2.12.
Solid tensor products

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
F,G
be
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules. Then we write

(3.2.48)

\displaystyle F\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}G

for the sheafification of the presheaf sending
(A,A^{+})
to

(3.2.49)

\displaystyle\left(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F)\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{A}}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),G)\right)(\ast),

where
\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{A}}
is the solid tensor product.

Remark 3.2.13
.

As a warning, we note that
F\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
might not be equal to
F
, because
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),F)
might not be solid. Even when starting with sheaves satisfying this solidity condition, it is not clear that the tensor product is associative or has any other good formal properties (we thank Peter Scholze for pointing this out to us).

3.3.
Sheaves of geometric origin

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of small v-sheaves and consider the sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras
\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}:=f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}
. We are going to study this sheaf under some assumptions.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a fiercely v-complete Huber pair and let
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
be strongly smooth. For
X=\operatorname{Spd}(P,P^{+})\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})=Y
, the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}
is a direct summand of a countably orthonormalizable Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules.

Proof.

Factor
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
as
(R,R^{+})\to(R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle,\overline{R^{+}\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle})\to(P,P^{+})
with the second map finite étale as in the definition. This exhibits
(P,P^{+})
as a finite locally free module over
R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle
, and thus an
R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle
-linear direct summand of
R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle^{\oplus N}
for some
N
, see
[
48
, Tag 00NX]
.

Write
Z=\operatorname{Spd}(R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle,\overline{R^{+}\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle})
. Then we claim that

(3.3.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y}\simeq\hat{\oplus}_{I}\mathcal{O}_{Y}

with
I=\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{k}
. The claim follows from Corollary
3.1.4
together with the fact that the formation of Tate algebras commutes with fiber products of adic spaces (see Section
3.1.7
) and Lemma
3.1.14
, which gives the v-completeness of Tate algebras over affinoid perfectoid
(A,A^{+})
. Since
X\to Z
is finite étale and
(R,R^{+})
is fiercely v-complete, it follows in a similar way that (here we are taking the algebraic tensor product)

(3.3.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y}\otimes_{H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y})}P.

Now since
P
is an
H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y})=R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle
-linear direct summand of
H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y})^{\oplus N}
for some
N
, it follows that
\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}
is a
\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y}
-linear direct summand of
\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y}^{\oplus N}
for some
N
, proving the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.3.2
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for
(R,R^{+})
a fiercely v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of small v-sheaves such that
X=\varinjlim_{n}U_{n}
, where
U_{n}\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
is given by
\operatorname{Spd}(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
with
(R,R^{+})\to(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})
strongly smooth. If the transition maps
P_{n+1}\to P_{n}
have dense image, then
\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}
is locally strongly countably Fréchet. If the transition maps
\underline{P_{n+1}}\to\underline{P_{n}}
are moreover compact, then
\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}
is locally strongly countably Fréchet of compact type.

Proof.

First of all
\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}
is a direct summand of a countably orthonormalizable Banach sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules by Lemma
3.3.1
. It follows from Lemma
3.1.14
and Lemma
3.1.6
that
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})\simeq P_{n}
, thus the transition maps
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
have dense image. Moreover, the transition maps
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})
are compact if the maps
\underline{P_{n+1}}\to\underline{P_{n}}
are compact. The lemma is thus proved if we show that the natural map

(3.3.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}\to\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}

is an isomorphism. We can check this on
(A,A^{+})\in\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})_{\mathrm{v}}
, and then

(3.3.4)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X_{\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})},\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle=\hom\left(X_{\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})},\mathbb{A}^{1,\lozenge}_{\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})}\right)

(3.3.5)

\displaystyle=\hom\left(\varinjlim_{n}U_{n,\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})},\mathbb{A}^{1,\lozenge}_{\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})}\right)

which identifies with
H^{0}\left(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}\right)=\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}\left(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}\right)
, proving the lemma.
∎

3.3.3.

Let
X\to Y,Z\to Y
be morphisms of small v-stacks. Then there is a natural cup product morphism

(3.3.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{\left(X\times_{Y}Z\right)/Y}.

given by the solidification (and then sheafification) of the usual cup product map (see e.g.
[
48
, Tag 0B6C]
).

Lemma 3.3.4
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
and
(R,R^{+})\to(Q,Q^{+})
be strongly smooth morphisms of Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. If we write
X=\operatorname{Spd}(P,P^{+})
and
Z=\operatorname{Spd}(Q,Q^{+})
, then the natural map

(3.3.7)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{X/Y})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Z/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{(X\times_{Y}Z)/Y})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Factor
(R,R^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
as

(R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle,\overline{R^{+}\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle})\xrightarrow{f}(P,P^{+})

with
f
finite étale and similarly factor
(R,R^{+})\to(Q,Q^{+})
as

(R\langle X_{k+1},\cdots,X_{k+j}\rangle,\overline{R^{+}\langle X_{k+1},\cdots,X_{k+j}\rangle})\xrightarrow{g}(Q,Q^{+})

with
g
finite étale. It follows from the discussion in §
3.1.7
and the preservation of Tate rings under fiber products of Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
that we can describe the fiber product
\operatorname{Spa}(Q,Q^{+})\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})}\operatorname{Spa}(P,P^{+})
as
\operatorname{Spa}(E,E^{+})
where

(3.3.8)

\displaystyle E=Q\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle\otimes_{R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{j+k}\rangle}P\langle X_{k+1},\cdots,X_{k+j}\rangle,

In particular, the natural map
(R,R^{+})\to(E,E^{+})
is strongly smooth, and so
(E,E^{+})
is v-complete by Lemma
3.1.14
. Moreover, since

(3.3.9)

\displaystyle\underline{R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}\rangle}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{R\langle X_{k+1},\cdots,X_{k+j}\rangle}\simeq\underline{R\langle X_{1},\cdots,X_{k+j}\rangle}

by Proposition
2.2.7
, it follows that
\underline{E}=\underline{P}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{Q}
. Thus by Lemma
3.1.6
the natural maps

(3.3.10)

\displaystyle\underline{P}

\displaystyle\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(X,\mathcal{O})

(3.3.11)

\displaystyle\underline{Q}

\displaystyle\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Z,\mathcal{O})

(3.3.12)

\displaystyle\underline{P}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}\underline{Q}

\displaystyle\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(X\times_{Y}Z,\mathcal{O})

are isomorphisms, proving the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.3.5
.

Let
X\to Y,Z\to Y
be morphisms of small v-stacks. If there is a v-cover
Y^{\prime}=\coprod_{j\in J}\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A^{+}_{j})\to Y
with
(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
such that: For each
j\in J
we have
Z_{\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A^{+}_{j})}\simeq\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
and
X_{\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A^{+}_{j})}\simeq\operatorname{Spd}(P,P^{+})
for
(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\to(R,R^{+})
and
(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\to(P,P^{+})
strongly smooth, then the natural map of (
3.3.6
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to prove this after basechanging to
Y^{\prime}
, so we will assume
Y=Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
. We can then check the statement of the proposition on
\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+})
points for
(B,B^{+})\in\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})_{\mathrm{v}}
and we may assume without loss of generality that
(B,B^{+})=(A,A^{+})
, noting that the assumptions of the lemma are stable under basechange. The lemma now follows from Lemma
3.3.4
.
∎

Proposition 3.3.6
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
X=\varinjlim_{n}U_{n}
, where
U_{n}\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
is given by
\operatorname{Spd}(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for some strongly smooth
(R,R^{+})\to(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})
. If the transition maps
P_{n+1}\to P_{n}
have dense image, then the natural map

(3.3.13)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{X/Y})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{X/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{(X\times_{Y}X)/Y})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.3.4
that the natural map

(3.3.14)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{(U_{n}\times_{Y}U_{n})/Y})

is an isomorphism. The lemma now follows by observing that

(3.3.15)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{X/Y})\simeq\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}),

see the proof of Lemma
3.3.2
, and applying Proposition
2.4.10
to commute the solid tensor product with the inverse limit in both factors.
∎

Proposition 3.3.7
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of small v-sheaves. Assume that there is a v-cover
Y^{\prime}=\coprod_{j\in J}\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A^{+}_{j})\to Y
with
(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
such that: For each
j\in J
we have
X_{\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A^{+}_{j})}=\varinjlim_{n}U_{n}
where
U_{n}\to\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})
is given by
\operatorname{Spd}(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spd}(P_{n,j},P_{n,j}^{+})
for some strongly smooth
(A_{j},A_{j}^{+})\to(P_{n,j},P_{n,j}^{+})
. If the transition maps
P_{n,j}\to P_{n+1,j}
have dense image, then the natural map of (
3.3.6
) (with
Z=X
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to prove this after basechanging to
Y^{\prime}
, so we will assume
Y=Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
. We can then check the statement of the proposition on
\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+})
points for
(B,B^{+})\in\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})_{\mathrm{v}}
, and we can as in the proof of Lemma
3.3.5
assume that
(B,B^{+})=(A,A^{+})
(using Lemma
2.4.5
). The statement now follows from Proposition
3.3.6
.
∎

3.3.8.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of small v-stacks. We will write
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X/Y}=(f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+})
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{X/Y}=\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X/Y}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
.

Lemma 3.3.9
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
X=\varinjlim_{n}U_{n}
, where
U_{n}\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
is given by
\operatorname{Spd}(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for some strongly smooth
(R,R^{+})\to(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})
. For
X^{\mathrm{rig}}:=\varinjlim_{n}\operatorname{Spa}(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})
, there are isomorphisms

(3.3.16)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X/Y})\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{H^{0}(X^{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+})}

(3.3.17)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{X/Y})\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{H^{0}(X^{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+})[\tfrac{1}{p}]}.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of the v-completeness of
(P_{n},P_{n}^{+})
, see Lemma
3.1.14
, and the fact (which follows as in the proof of Lemma
3.3.2
) that

(3.3.18)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X/Y})=\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{+}_{U_{n}/Y})

(3.3.19)

\displaystyle H^{0}(X^{\mathrm{rig}},\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+})=\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}(\operatorname{Spa}(P_{n},P_{n}^{+}),\mathcal{O}_{X}^{+}).

∎

4.
Families of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups

In this section we discuss the theory of families of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups. In §
4.1
, we discuss families of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups over sousperfectoid adic spaces and rigid spaces over non-archimedean extensions of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. When the base is the adic spectrum of a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we discuss the classification of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups in terms of Hodge–Tate triples, following Fargues
[
14
]
. In §
4.2
, we define
p
-divisible v-groups over general small v-stacks and construct a functor from Hodge–Tate triples to
p
-divisible v-groups in this generality. In §
4.3
, we introduce the category of finite height
p
-divisible v-groups and relate them to locally analytic character data.

4.1.
Families of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups

In this section, we discuss families of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups, following Fargues
[
14
,
15
]
.

4.1.1.

Let
Y
be a sousperfectoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or a rigid space over a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We will consider relative commutative groups
H\to Y
in the category of smooth adic spaces over
Y
, see
[
13
, Section IV.4.1]
. These have a Lie algebra
\operatorname{Lie}H
, see
[
24
, p. 10]
, which is functorial in
H
. To define a logarithm map, we need to restrict to groups which are
p
-divisible in a precise sense, see
[
15
, Definition 4.1]
. We will present this definition following
[
24
, Section 3.2]
.

4.1.2.

Recall from
[
24
, Definition 3.2.3]
that a subspace
T\subset H
is called
topologically
p
-torsion
if for any open subspace
U\subset H
containing the image of the unit section, and any quasicompact open subspace
T_{0}\subset T
there is an
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
[p^{n}](T_{0})\subset U
, where
[p^{n}]:H\to H
is the multiplication by
p^{n}
map.

In the rest of §
4.1.2
, we explain the statement of
[
24
, Proposition 3.2.4]
: There is an open
Y
-subgroup
H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle\subset H
which is topologically
p
-torsion and is maximal among topologically
p
-torsion open
Y
-subgroups of
H
.
10
10

10

Note that
[
24
, Proposition 3.2.4]
writes
\widehat{H}
for what we call
H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle
; we instead follow
[
25
, Definition 2.6]
.
Moreover, there is a functorial map of
Y
-groups

(4.1.1)

\displaystyle\log_{H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle}:H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle\to\operatorname{Lie}H,

which induces the identity map on Lie algebras. This fits in an exact sequence

(4.1.2)

\displaystyle 0\to H[p^{\infty}]\to H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle\to\operatorname{Lie}H,

where
H[p^{\infty}]=\bigcup_{n}H[p^{n}]
. If
[p]:H\to H
is étale, then so is
\log_{H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle}
, and if
[p]:H\to H
is surjective, then so is
\log_{H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle}
. Finally, the morphism of v-sheaves in groups
H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle^{\lozenge}\to H^{\lozenge}
over
Y^{\lozenge}
can be identified with the evaluation at
1
map
\Hom_{Y^{\lozenge}}(\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{Y},H^{\lozenge})\to H^{\lozenge}
. In particular, this gives
H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle^{\lozenge}
the structure of a
\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-module.

4.1.3.

Let
Y
be a sousperfectoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or a rigid space over a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
H\to Y
be a commutative smooth relative group in adic spaces. Recall that
H\to Y
is a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group
if
H\langle p^{\infty}\rangle=H
and if
[p]
is finite étale surjective, see
[
15
, Definition 4.1]
. Note that
[
18
]
uses the terminology analytic
p
-divisible group.

Example 4.1.4
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and consider the rigid analytic fiber
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
over
Y
of the formal multiplicative group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. This is a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group (e.g. by Lemma
4.1.6
). The logarith map
\operatorname{log}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}
is defined by the usual power series expansion

\log(1+t)=\sum_{n\geq 1}(-1)^{n+1}\frac{t^{n}}{n}.

For
\varpi=2p
, the map
\operatorname{log}^{\lozenge}
is an isomorphism from
\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|)
, the ball of radius
|\varpi|
around
1
in
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}^{\lozenge}
, to
\mathbb{B}(0,|\varpi|)
, the ball of radius
|\varpi|
around
0
in
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\lozenge}
, with inverse defined by the usual exponential series

\exp(t)=\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{1}{n!}t^{n}.

4.1.5.

Recall that a
p
-divisible group over a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
(in the sense of
[
44
, Section 2.2]
) is a relative commutative group functor
\mathcal{H}\to X
such that
[p]:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}
is an epimorphism in the fpqc topology, such that
\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{H}[p^{n}]\to\mathcal{H}
is an isomorphism, and such that
\mathcal{H}[p^{n}]\to X
is representable in finite locally free group schemes for all
n
. Note that
\mathcal{H}
might not be representable by a formal scheme (although it often is, see
[
44
, Lemma 3.1.1]
). We will write
T_{p}\mathcal{H}
for the
p
-adic Tate module
\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{H}[p^{n}]
, where the transition maps are induced by multiplication by
p
. The functor
T_{p}\mathcal{H}\to X
is representable in flat affine morphisms over
R
, see
[
44
, Proposition 3.3.1]
.

Lemma 4.1.6
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
be a sousperfectoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or a rigid space over a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
\mathcal{H}\to\operatorname{Spf}R^{+}
be a
p
-divisible group. If
(R,R^{+})
is uniform, then the rigid generic fiber
11
11

11

Which is defined because
R^{+}
is a ring of definition, since
R
is uniform.
in the sense of
[
44
, Section 2.2]
,
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}
, is a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group over
Y
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
44
, Proposition 3.4.2.(ii)]
and its proof, as we will now explain. Let
H:=\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}
. First of all, it follows from that proof that
H\to Y
is representable by a relative group in adic spaces. It is moreover established that
H
comes equipped with an exact sequence (where
\operatorname{Lie}H=\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{H}\otimes_{R^{+}}\mathbb{G}_{a}
)

(4.1.3)

\displaystyle 0\to H[p^{\infty}]\to H\xrightarrow{\log_{H}}\operatorname{Lie}H.

Furthermore, there is an open subset
U\subset H
containing the identity section such that the restriction of
\log_{H}
to
U
induces an isomorphism between
U
and
\log_{H}(U)
. On top of that,
\log_{H}(U)
is an open subset of
\operatorname{Lie}H
containing the identity section, and it follows that
U
is smooth over
Y
. Finally it is shown in the proof of
[
44
, Proposition 3.4.2.(ii)]
that
H
is covered by the inverse images under
[p^{n}]
of
U
, and that
[p]:H\to H
is relatively representable and finite locally free.

It remains to show that
[p]:H\to H
is finite étale and surjective. By the discussion above, for an affinoid open
W=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\subset H
, the inverse image
V=[p]^{-1}W=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})
for some finite locally free
R
-algebra
B
. Since
[p]
is clearly a quasi-torsor for
H[p]
, we see that
V\times_{W}V\simeq V\times H[p]
over
W
. But
V\times_{W}V
is given by
\operatorname{Spa}(D,D^{+})
where
D=B\otimes_{R}B
, and
H[p]
is finite étale over
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
since
p
is invertible in
R
. Thus it follows from faithfully flat descent that
B
is a finite étale
R
-algebra, showing that
[p]
is finite étale and surjective.
∎

4.1.7.

Let
\pi:H\to Y
be a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group as above. Then the
p^{n}
-torsion
H[p^{n}]
of
H
is a locally free of finite rank sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-modules on
Y_{\text{\'{e}t}}
. The multiplication by
p
map is surjective and thus defines a surjection
H[p^{n+1}]\to H[p^{n}]
with kernel
H[p]
. Using this observation, we define a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
Y
by
T_{p}H:=\varprojlim_{n}H[p^{n+1}]
, the inverse limit being taken in
Y_{\mathrm{v}}^{\lozenge}
.

4.1.8.
Classification I

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
be a sousperfectoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or a rigid space over a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Definition 4.1.9
.

We define the category
\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y)
of
étale Hodge–Tate triples
to be the category of triples
(T,W,\alpha)
, where
T
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
Y^{\lozenge}_{\mathrm{v}}
, where
W
is an étale vector bundle on
Y
, and where
\alpha:W^{\lozenge}\to T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\lozenge}}
is a morphism of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\lozenge}}
-modules on
Y^{\lozenge}_{\mathrm{v}}
. Associated to a triple
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y)
we consider the v-sheaf
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{L}}
of commutative groups over
Y^{\lozenge}
given by the fiber product

(4.1.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}.}

\lrcorner

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes\log^{\lozenge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

Proposition 4.1.10
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)
and
H_{\mathcal{L}}
be as above. If
(R,R^{+})
is fiercely v-complete, then there is a unique
p
-divisible rigid analytic group
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\to Y
such that
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{rig},\lozenge}
is isomorphic to
H_{\mathcal{L}}
.

In the proof of Proposition
4.1.10
, we will reduce to the case that the vector bundle
W
is trivial.

Lemma 4.1.11
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)
and
H=H_{\mathcal{L}}
be as above. If
(R,R^{+})
is fiercely v-complete and
W
is free, then there are open subgroups
H_{n}\subset H
indexed by
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
H=\bigcup_{n}H_{n}
and such that
H_{n}=\operatorname{Spd}(A_{n},A_{n}^{+})
with
(R,R^{+})\to(A_{n},A_{n}^{+})
strongly smooth.

Proof.

Let
\varpi=2p
and for
n\geq 0
let

(4.1.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|^{1/p^{n}})\subset\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}

denote the open subset defined by the inequality
|T|^{p^{i}}\leq|\varpi|
, and let

(4.1.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}(0,|\frac{\varpi}{p^{n}}|)\subset\mathbb{G}_{a}

be the closed unit disk of radius
|\frac{\varpi}{p^{n}}|
. Consider the commutative diagram

(4.1.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|^{1/p^{n}})^{\lozenge}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}T(-1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|)^{\lozenge}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}T(-1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}(0,|\frac{\varpi}{p^{n}}|)^{\lozenge}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}T(-1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{B}(0,|\varpi|)^{\lozenge}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}T(-1)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z\mapsto z^{p^{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{log}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\mapsto p^{n}t}

The right vertical arrow and bottom horizontal arrow are isomorphisms (see Example
4.1.4
). Since the top horizontal arrow is finite étale, the left vertical arrow is also finite étale. We now consider an affinoid open neighborhood of
U\subseteq W
of
U
such that
U^{\lozenge}
is contained in
\mathbb{B}(0,|\varpi|)^{\lozenge}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}T(-1)
. We define
H_{0}^{\lozenge}
to be the preimage of
U
in
\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|)^{\lozenge}
(so that
H_{0}^{\lozenge}
is isomorphic by
\log\otimes 1
to
U
). We then define
H_{n}^{\lozenge}
to be the preimage of
H_{0}^{\lozenge}
in
{\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|^{1/p^{n}})^{\lozenge}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}T(-1)}
. It is a finite étale cover of
H_{0}^{\lozenge}
sitting in the subdiagram of (
4.1.7
)

(4.1.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{n}^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{0}^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\frac{1}{p^{n}}U)^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\lozenge}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\mapsto p^{n}t}

We thus have
H^{\lozenge}=\bigcup_{n}H_{n}^{\lozenge}
, and each
H_{n}^{\lozenge}
is finite étale over
U^{\lozenge}
via the map
(t\mapsto p^{n}t)\circ\log
. By the equivalence of the finite étale sites of
U
and
U^{\lozenge}
, see
[
46
, Lemma 15.6]
, each
H_{n}^{\lozenge}
is of the form
\operatorname{Spd}(A_{n},A_{n}^{+})
where
A_{n}
is a finite étale
A=\mathcal{O}(U)
-algebra. Now, since
W
is trivializable, we may choose
U
to be an affinoid ball so that
(R,R^{+})\to(\mathcal{O}(U),\mathcal{O}^{+}(U))\to(A_{n},A_{n}^{+})
exhibits
(R,R^{+})\to(A_{n},A_{n}^{+})
as strongly smooth.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.1.10
.

It follows from Lemma
4.1.11
and Lemma
3.1.14
that, analytically locally on
Y
, we can write
H_{\mathcal{L}}\to Y^{\lozenge}
as
H^{\mathrm{rig}}_{\mathcal{L}}\to Y
with
H^{\mathrm{rig}}_{\mathcal{L}}\to Y
locally fiercely v-complete. By the fully faithfulness proved in Lemma
3.1.12
, this also holds globally on
Y
since the gluing datum for the v-sheaf
H_{\mathcal{L}}
induces a gluing datum of adic spaces. By the same fully faithfulness and the compatibility of
X\mapsto X^{\lozenge}
with products, we see that
H^{\mathrm{rig}}_{\mathcal{L}}\to Y
has the structure of a relative smooth commutative group in adic spaces, and it suffices to show that it is a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group.

For this, we note that
H_{\mathcal{L}}=\Hom_{Y^{\lozenge}}(\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{Y^{\lozenge}},H_{\mathcal{L}})
since this is true for the other three terms in the fiber product diagram. Moreover, the natural map
[p]:H_{\mathcal{L}}\to H_{\mathcal{L}}
is (representable in) surjective finite étale morphisms since this is true for the other three terms in the diagram. By Lemma
3.1.12
and
[
46
, Lemma 15.6]
, this implies that
[p]:H^{\mathrm{rig}}_{\mathcal{L}}\to H^{\mathrm{rig}}_{\mathcal{L}}
is finite étale surjective. Thus
H^{\mathrm{rig}}_{\mathcal{L}}\to Y
is a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group.
∎

The construction
\mathcal{L}\to H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{rig}}
defines a functor from
\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y)
to the category of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups over
Y
.

4.1.12.
Classification II

A result of Fargues, see
[
14
, Théorème 0.1]
, tells us that the functor
\mathcal{L}\mapsto H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{rig}}
is an equivalence of categories when
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
with
K
a field. In the forthcoming PhD thesis of Gerth
[
19
]
, this will be generalized to work over an arbitrary perfectoid
Y
.

4.2.
Families over small v-stacks

Let
Y
be a small v-stack (see §
3.1
).

Definition 4.2.1
.

We say that a v-sheaf of commutative groups
H\to Y
is a
p
-divisible v-group
if for all affinoid perfectoid spaces
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})\to Y
, the base change
H_{Y^{\prime}}\to Y^{\prime}
is (the diamond associated to) a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group over
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
.

4.2.2.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
H\to Y
be a
p
-divisible v-group.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

There is a locally free sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules
\operatorname{Lie}H
and a short exact sequence

(4.2.1)

\displaystyle 0\to H[p^{\infty}]\to H\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Log}_{H}}\operatorname{Lie}H\to 0

of v-sheaves in abelian groups on
Y_{\mathrm{v}}
. The formation of
(\operatorname{Lie}H,\operatorname{Log}_{H})
is functorial in morphisms of
p
-divisible v-groups over
Y
and commutes with pullback along
Y^{\prime}\to Y
.

Proof.

By v-descent, it suffices to prove this when
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
with
(A,A^{+})
affinoid perfectoid. But then it is established in §
4.1.2
.
∎

4.2.4.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
, where
(R,R^{+})
is a fiercely v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Lemma 4.2.5
.

If
H\to\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
is a
p
-divisible v-group such that
\operatorname{Lie}H
comes from an analytic vector bundle over
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
, then there is a unique commutative group
H^{\mathrm{rig}}\to\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
in smooth analytic adic spaces over
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
whose associated diamond is
H
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.1.10
and its proof, using Lemma
4.2.3
.
∎

This gives us a theory of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups over arbitrary fiercely v-complete Huber pairs over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Lemma 4.2.6
.

Let
H\to Y
be a v-sheaf of commutative groups. If there is a v-cover
Y^{\prime}\to Y
such that
H_{Y^{\prime}}\to Y^{\prime}
is (the diamond associated to) a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group, then
H\to Y
is a
p
-divisible v-group.

Proof.

The property that
\Hom_{Y}(\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{Y},H)\to H
is an isomorphism can be checked v-locally, because it is a map of v-sheaves. By
[
46
, Proposition 10.11.(iii)]
, the same is true for the property that
[p]
is finite étale surjective. The existence of the logarithm as in Lemma
4.2.3
can also be proved v-locally, and the representability over perfectoid spaces follows from the existence of the logarithm as in the proof of Lemma
4.2.5
.
∎

If
Y
is a sousperfectoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or a rigid space over a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and
H\to Y
is a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group, then
H^{\lozenge}\to Y^{\lozenge}
is a
p
-divisible v-group; this follows as in Lemma
4.2.6
. We note however that
H\mapsto H^{\lozenge}
is generally not essentially surjective.

4.2.7.
Classification

Let
Y
be a small v-stack.

Definition 4.2.8
.

Let
\mathcal{HT}(Y)
be the category of Hodge–Tate triples consisting of triples
(T,W,\alpha)
, where
T
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
Y_{v}
, where
W
is a locally free sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules on
Y_{\mathrm{v}}
, and where
\alpha:W\to T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
is a morphism. For
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}(Y)
, we write
H_{\mathcal{L}}
for the fiber product

(4.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathcal{L}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}.}

\lrcorner

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1\otimes\log^{\lozenge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

Lemma 4.2.9
.

The functor
\mathcal{L}\mapsto H_{\mathcal{L}}
commutes with basechange along
Y^{\prime}\to Y
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that tensor products of sheaves of modules and fiber products of sheaves both commute with base change.
∎

Lemma 4.2.10
.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack. If
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{HT}(Y)
, then the v-sheaf in commutative groups
H_{\mathcal{L}}\to Y
is a
p
-divisible v-group.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.2.9
, it suffices to prove representability when
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
with
(A,A^{+})\in{\mathrm{Perfd}}
. In this case, the sheaf
W
of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules is free étale locally on
Y
by
[
35
, Theorem 3.5.8]
, and the result is Proposition
4.1.10
.
∎

4.2.11.

If
Y
is a locally spatial diamond in the sense of
[
46
, Definition 11.17]
, then we define the full subcategory
\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y)\subset\mathcal{HT}(Y)
of étale Hodge–Tate triples to be those triples
(T,W,\alpha)
with
W
an étale vector bundle (using the étale site of
[
46
, Definition 14.1]
). If
X
is a sousperfectoid adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
or a rigid space over a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, then, by
[
46
, Lemma 15.6]
,
\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X^{\lozenge})=\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X)
, where the latter was defined in Definition
4.1.9
.

Conjecture 1
.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack. The functor
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\to H_{\mathcal{L}}
is an equivalence of categories between
\mathcal{HT}(Y)
and the category of
p
-divisible
v
-groups over
Y
.

Note that both categories are the evaluation of a v-stack on
Y
: For
\mathcal{HT}(Y)
this is clear from the definition and for
p
-divisible v-groups this is Lemma
4.2.6
. Thus it suffices to prove Conjecture
1
for
Y
a (strictly totally disconnected) perfectoid space; this will be done in the forthcoming PhD thesis of Gerth
[
19
]
.

4.3.
p
-divisible v-groups of finite height

Let
Y
be a small v-stack.

Definition 4.3.1
.

We say that a triple
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}(Y)
has
finite height
if
\alpha
is injective. Note that this condition is stable under pullback along
Y^{\prime}\to Y
. We define
\mathcal{HT}_{\mathrm{fh}}(Y)\subset\mathcal{HT}(Y)
to be the full subcategory of triples
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)
of finite height. We say that a
p
-divisible v-group
H\to Y
has
finite height
if
H=H_{\mathcal{L}}
with
\mathcal{L}
of finite height.

If
H
arises as the generic fiber of a
p
-divisible group, then it is of finite height, see Proposition
4.3.4
. As a special case, this includes the generic fibers of
p
-divisible (equivalently, finite height) formal groups; this explains our choice of terminology.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}(Y)
. The triple
\mathcal{L}
has finite height if and only if
\alpha
realizes
W
as a local direct summand of
T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
. In this case,
\mathrm{coker}(\alpha)
is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module.

Proof.

The if direction is trivial. Thus, let us assume
\alpha
is injective. We may assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
is perfectoid and that
W
has constant rank over
Y
. Via taking global sections, the category of locally free of finite rank
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules is equivalent to the category of finite projective
A
modules, see
[
35
, Theorem 3.5.8]
. Let
P=H^{0}(Y,W)
and
Q=H^{0}(Y,T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y})
be the associated finite projective
A
modules, and let
f:P\hookrightarrow Q
be the associated map. By testing on geometric points, we find that for any
x\in\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
, the map
f\otimes_{A}\kappa(x):P\otimes_{A}\kappa(x)\rightarrow Q\otimes_{A}\kappa(x)
is also injective. Thus
\operatorname{Coker}(f)\otimes_{A}\kappa(x)
has constant rank and, by
[
34
, Proposition 2.8]
,
\operatorname{Coker}(f)
is finite projective. Thus the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow P\rightarrow Q\rightarrow\operatorname{Coker}f\rightarrow 0

is split, and any associated direct sum decomposition
Q=P\oplus\operatorname{Coker}f
induces the desired direct sum decomposition of sheaves

T(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}=Q\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{Y}=(P\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{Y})\oplus(\mathrm{coker}(f)\otimes_{A}\mathcal{O}_{Y})=W\oplus W^{\prime}.

∎

4.3.3.

The following proposition shows that the adic generic fiber of a (classical)
p
-divisible group is of finite height. Recall that given a
p
-divisible group
\mathcal{H}
over a
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, there is a Serre-dual
p
-divisible group
\mathcal{H}^{\vee}
and a Hodge–Tate map
T_{p}\mathcal{H}^{\vee}\to\omega_{\mathcal{H}}
, see for example Section
7.1.5
below.

Proposition 4.3.4
.

Let
(R,R^{+})/(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be a fiercely v-complete Huber pair. If
\mathcal{H}
is a
p
-divisible group over
\operatorname{Spf}R^{+}
, then there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{H}_{\eta}^{\lozenge}\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{\mathcal{L}}\textrm{ for }\mathcal{L}={(T_{p}\mathcal{H}_{\eta},\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{H}\otimes_{R^{+}}\mathcal{O}_{Y},\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{H}\otimes_{R^{+}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\hookrightarrow T_{p}\mathcal{H}_{\eta}(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y})}.

Here
\alpha:\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{H}\otimes_{R^{+}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\hookrightarrow T_{p}\mathcal{H}_{\eta}(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
is the dual Hodge–Tate map.

Proof.

We follow the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 5.2.1]
. Let
T_{p}(H)
be the
p
-adic Tate module of
H=H_{\eta}
, which is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
that we can identify with
\left(T_{p}\mathcal{H}^{\vee}\right)_{\eta}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. Let
H^{\prime}=T_{p}(H)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}_{R}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,R}
and note that there is a natural map
H\to H^{\prime}
of
p
-divisible rigid analytic groups over
\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
which induces an isomorphism
T_{p}(H)\xrightarrow{\sim}T_{p}(H^{\prime})
on
p
-adic Tate modules. The induced map
\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{H}[\tfrac{1}{p}]=\operatorname{Lie}H\to\operatorname{Lie}H^{\prime}=T_{p}(H)(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})}
is moreover identified with the Hodge–Tate map. Thus we get a commutative diagram

(4.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{H}\otimes_{R^{+}}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{p}\mathcal{H}_{\eta}(-1)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})}}
