and the proposition comes down to showing that it is in fact Cartesian. This can be proved as in the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 5.2.1]
.
∎

4.3.5.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack.

Definition 4.3.6
.

A locally analytic character datum over
Y
is a triple
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
, where
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
Y
, where
V
is a locally free of finite rank sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules and
\gamma
is a surjection
\gamma:\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to V
. We will write
\mathcal{CD}(Y)
for the category of locally analytic character data over
Y
. For
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
we define the character v-group to be the fiber product

(4.3.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{Y}(\Lambda_{Y},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(V,\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{Y}(\Lambda_{Y},\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Log}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{\ast}}

There is a contravariant functor
\mathcal{CD}(Y)\to\mathcal{HT}_{\mathrm{fh}}(Y)
given by

(4.3.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)\mapsto\mathcal{X}^{\vee}=(\Lambda^{\ast}(1),V^{\ast},\gamma^{\ast}),

which is an equivalence of categories with inverse

(4.3.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\mapsto\mathcal{L}^{\vee}=(T^{\ast}(1),W^{\ast},\alpha^{\ast}).

Lemma 4.3.7
.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
. There is a functorial isomorphism

(4.3.5)

\displaystyle H_{\mathcal{X}}\to H_{\mathcal{X}^{\vee}}.

In particular,
H_{\mathcal{X}}
is a
p
-divisible v-group, and every finite height
p
-divisible
v
-group is of the form
H_{\mathcal{X}}
for some character datum
\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

Observe that

(4.3.6)

\displaystyle\Hom_{Y}(\Lambda_{Y},\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge})

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}

(4.3.7)

\displaystyle\Hom_{Y}(\Lambda_{Y},\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge})

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}

(4.3.8)

\displaystyle\Hom_{Y}(V,\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge})

\displaystyle=V^{\ast}.

The first part of the lemma is now a direct consequence of the definition of
\mathcal{X}^{\vee}
, together with Definition
4.3.6
and Definition
4.2.1
. The second part of the lemma follows from Lemma
4.2.10
.
∎

4.3.8.
Moduli

Fix an integer
h\geq 0
and an integer
0\leq d\leq h
and let
Y
be a small v-stack. Then there is a full subcategory
\mathcal{LA}_{h,d}(Y)\subset\mathcal{HT}_{\mathrm{fh}}(Y)
consisting of those triples
(T,W,\alpha)
where
T
is locally free of rank
h
and
W
is locally free of rank
d
. Note that the assignment
Y\mapsto\mathcal{LA}_{h,d}(Y)\subset\mathcal{HT}_{\mathrm{fh}}(Y)
is a substack for the v-topology. Consider the natural action of
\underline{\operatorname{GL}_{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
on
\operatorname{Gr}_{h,d}^{\lozenge}
, where
\operatorname{Gr}_{h,d}
is the (schematic) Grassmannian of
d
-planes in
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus h}
.

Lemma 4.3.9
.

There is an equivalence of v-stacks on
\operatorname{Perf}

(4.3.9)

\displaystyle\left[\operatorname{Gr}_{h,d}^{\lozenge}/\underline{\operatorname{GL}_{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\right]\to\mathcal{LA}_{h,d}.

Proof.

It suffices to define the equivalence on affinoid perfectoid test objects
(A,A^{+})
, where it is essentially a tautology once we use the fact that v-vector bundles on affinoid perfectoid spaces descend to analytic vector bundles, see
[
35
, Theorem 3.5.8]
.
∎

5.
Sheaves of functions and distributions

In §
5.1
, we show that sheaves of functions on
p
-divisible v-groups over small v-stacks have good functional analytic properties, see Proposition
5.1.1
. In §
5.2
, we introduce spaces of locally analytic functions associated with locally analytic character data, and show that these have good functional analytic properties, see Proposition
5.2.9
.

5.1.
Functions on
p
-divisible v-groups.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}(Y)
be a Hodge–Tate triple. Consider the
p
-divisible v-group
H_{\mathcal{L}}\xrightarrow{f}Y
constructed from
\mathcal{L}
as in Definition
4.2.8
and Lemma
4.2.10
. Write
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}=f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}}
for the pushforward on v-sites, and let
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y},\mathcal{O}_{Y})
be its dual.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

If
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
W
is free, then the following hold:

(1)

For all morphisms of fiercely v-complete Huber pairs
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
, the natural map (where
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+})
)

(5.1.1)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})

is an isomorphism.

(2)

The natural map

(5.1.2)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}\times\mathcal{L}}/Y})

is an isomorphism.

(3)

The natural map

(5.1.3)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\underline{R}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}),\underline{R})

is an isomorphism.

(4)

The natural map

(5.1.4)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R}}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}\times\mathcal{L}}/Y})

is an isomorphism.

For
Y
an arbitrary small v-stack, the following hold:

(5)

The sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}
is reflexive.

(6)

The sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}
is locally strongly countably Fréchet of compact type in the sense of Definition
3.2.8
.

(7)

The natural map

(5.1.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}\times\mathcal{L}}/Y}.

is an isomorphism.

(8)

The natural map

(5.1.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to\left(\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\right)^{\ast}

is an isomorphism.

We start by proving a geometric lemma.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y^{\lozenge})
and let
H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{rig}}=H\to Y
be the corresponding commutative smooth group in adic spaces (see Lemma
4.2.5
). If
W
is free, then there is an increasing union of open affinoid
Y
-subgroups
H_{n}\subset H
indexed by
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
H=\bigcup_{n}H_{n}
and such that:

•

For each
n
, there is a finite étale map
H_{n}\rightarrow U
, where
U
is a closed ball in
W
. In particular,
\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
is a (topological) direct summand of the countably orthonormalizable Banach
R
-module
\mathcal{O}(U)^{\oplus m_{n}}
for some
m_{n}
.

•

For
n<n^{\prime}
the restriction map
\mathcal{O}(H_{n^{\prime}})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
has dense image and the map
\underline{\mathcal{O}(H_{n^{\prime}})}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{O}(H_{n})}
is compact in the sense of Section
2.3.2
.

Proof.

Following the proof and notation of Lemma
4.1.11
, we will write
H_{n}=\bigcup_{n}H_{n}
where each
H_{n}
is finite étale over a ball
U\subset W
. Then
H_{n}=\operatorname{Spa}(A_{n},A_{n}^{+})
, where
A_{n}
is a finite étale
A=\mathcal{O}(U)
-algebra. In particular,
A_{n}
is a finite projective
A
-module, so a direct summand of
A^{\oplus m_{n}}
for some
m_{n}
(see the proof of Lemma
3.3.2
). Now, since
W
is trivializable, we may choose
U
to be an affinoid ball, so that
A
is orthonormalizable and we obtain the claim (2) on the structure of
A_{n}
.

For the claims about dense image and compactness, note that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{n}\cdot\log}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{n+1}\cdot\log}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

realizes
H_{n}
as a clopen subset of
H_{n+1}\times_{U}{pU}
. Thus
\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
is a direct summand of
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1}\times_{U}{pU})
as a topological
R
-module. We find that
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
can be factored as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1}\times_{U}pU)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}(H_{n})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}(U)^{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}(pU)^{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus}

where the middle vertical arrow is obtained by tensoring the left vertical arrow over
\mathcal{O}(U)
along the restriction map
\mathcal{O}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)
.

In particular, to see that
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
has dense image, we claim it suffices to see that
\mathcal{O}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)
has dense image. Indeed, choosing an idempotent
e
projecting from
\mathcal{O}(U)^{m}
onto
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})
, we see that image of
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
is dense because
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1}\times_{U}pU)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
is a surjection and
\mathcal{O}(U)^{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)^{m}
has dense image thus
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1}\times_{U}pU)
also has dense image as it can be identified with
e\mathcal{O}(U)^{m}\rightarrow e\mathcal{O}(pU)^{m}
. Now, we obtain the desired density because
\mathcal{O}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)
evidently has dense image: Choosing coordinates, it is the natural inclusion
R\langle T_{1},\ldots T_{d}\rangle\rightarrow R\langle\frac{T_{1}}{p}\ldots,\frac{T_{d}}{p}\rangle
, whose image contain the polynomials
R[T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}]
, which are a dense subset.

Similarly, to see
\mathcal{O}(H_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{n})
is compact, since it is obtained from
\mathcal{O}(U)^{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)^{m}
by pre-composition with the inclusion of a direct summand and post-composition with projection onto a direct summand, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{O}(U)^{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)^{m}
is compact. Since a finite direct sum of compact maps is compact, it thus suffices to show
\mathcal{O}(U)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(pU)
is compact. To prove this, we can moreover reduce to the case that
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
by Lemma
2.3.3
, and then the result is well known in classical functional analysis; with the present condensed definitions see, e.g.,
[
40
, Remark 4.9]
.
∎

Corollary 5.1.3
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y^{\lozenge})
and let
H=H_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{rig}}\to Y
be the corresponding commutative smooth group in adic spaces (see Lemma
4.2.5
). If
W
is free, then
\underline{\mathcal{O}(H)}
is a strongly countably Fréchet
\underline{R}
-module of compact type.

Proof of Proposition
5.1.1
.

Part (1) is a direct consequence of Lemma
3.2.9
and Lemma
5.1.2
. Part (2) follows from Proposition
3.3.6
and Lemma
5.1.2
. Part (3) follows from Lemma
3.2.3
and part (1). Part (4) follows from parts (2) and (3), in combination with Corollary
5.1.3
and Corollary
2.4.11
.

For the rest of the proposition, we can reduce to the case that
T
and
W
are trivial and that
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
is affinoid perfectoid. Part (6) is then a direct consequence of Lemma
3.3.2
and Lemma
5.1.2
. Part (5) follows from Corollary
3.2.11
and part (7) from Proposition
3.3.7
. Finally, part (8) follows by applying (3) and (4) to affinoid perfectoid
(A,A^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
.
∎

5.1.4.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack, let
\mathcal{L}=(T,W,\alpha)\in\mathcal{HT}(Y)
and let
H=H_{\mathcal{L}}
be the corresponding
p
-divisible v-group. Using part (7) of Proposition
5.1.1
, we see that the Künneth map

(5.1.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{H/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{H/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{\left(H\times_{Y}H\right)/Y}

is an isomorphism. This means that we can use the group structure (given by the multiplication map and the identity section) to equip
\mathcal{O}_{H/Y}
with the structure of a commutative solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra.
12
12

12

We define a commutative solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra to be a co-commutative co-group object in the category of commutative solid
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras with the solid tensor product. It follows in the usual way that for a commutative group
H\to Y
satisfying the Künneth formula for the solid tensor product, the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{H/Y}
is a solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{H/Y}
be
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{H/Y},\mathcal{O}_{Y})
. Then by part (8) of Proposition
5.1.1
, we see that the natural map

(5.1.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{H/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{H/Y}\to\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{H/Y}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{H/Y},\mathcal{O}_{Y})

is an isomorphism. This means that the commutative solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{H/Y}
induces a commutative solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{D}_{H/Y}
.

5.2.
Locally analytic functions

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum. We are going to define a sheaf
\mathcal{O}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}
of functions that extend locally along
\gamma
to an open neighborhood in
V
, and show that it is a subsheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
. We define

\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}:=\varinjlim_{(n,U)}\mathcal{O}_{U/Y},

where the colimit is over pairs
(n,U)
where
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
U
is an open neighborhood of the image of
\Lambda
in
\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times V
(under the product of
\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda/p^{n}
and
\gamma|_{\Lambda}
). We let

\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}:=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}},\mathcal{O}_{Y}).

(2)

Let
\Lambda
be a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system over
Y
. We write
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}:=\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{Id}_{\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}}-\mathrm{la}}
, i.e., the sheaf of locally analytic functions attached to the character datum
(\Lambda,\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y},\mathrm{Id}_{\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}}).

5.2.2.

The assignment
(\Lambda,V,\gamma)\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
is contravariantly functorial in
(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

In the notation of Definition
5.2.1
-(1), suppose
U_{0}
is an open subsheaf of
V
containing
\gamma(\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})
and contained in
p^{-N}\gamma(\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})
for some
N>0
. Then, the subsheaf
U_{0,n}
of
\Lambda/p^{n}\times_{Y}V
consisting of pairs
(\overline{\lambda},v)
where
\gamma(\lambda)-v\in p^{n}U_{0}
for one, equivalently any, local lift
\lambda
of
\overline{\lambda}
, is open. Moreover, there is a natural isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\to\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,n}/Y}.

Proof.

To verify that
U_{0,n}
is an open subsheaf, it suffices to show that
U_{0,n}
is open after restriction to any affinoid perfectoid test object
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
where
\Lambda\simeq\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{h}
is trivial and
V\simeq\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}^{d}
. We thus assume
Y=Y^{\prime}
and fix such trivializations. For any
n\geq 0
, we may fix elements
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{p^{nh}}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}
that map bijectively onto
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}
. Then
U_{0,n}
is the disjoint union of the open subsheaves

\{\overline{\lambda_{i}}\}\times(\gamma(\lambda_{i})+p^{n}U_{0})\subseteq\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times_{Y}V.

To show the second statement, we observe that
U_{0,n}
is an open neighborhood of the image of
\Lambda
in
\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times V
. Thus there is a restriction map

(5.2.1)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,n}/Y}\to\varinjlim_{(n,U)}\mathcal{O}_{U/Y}.

We can show it is an isomorphism after restricting to any affinoid perfectoid
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
where
\Lambda
and
V
are trivial. We thus assume
Y=Y^{\prime}
and fix such trivializations. Then, it suffices to show that for each
(U,n)
as in Definition
5.2.1
-(1), there exists
m\geq n
such that
U_{0,m}
is contained in the pre-image of
U
under
\Lambda/p^{m}\Lambda\times V\to\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times V
. Equivalently, that the image of
U_{0,m}
in
\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times V
is contained in
U
for some
m
.

For this, we note that
U_{0,m}
is the image in
\Lambda/p^{m}\Lambda\times V
of
\Lambda\times p^{m}U_{0}
(under the map given by the product of
\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda/p^{n}
and
\gamma|_{\Lambda}
in the first factor).Thus the image of
U_{0,m}
in
\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times V
is also the image of
\Lambda\times p^{m}U_{0}
. But these clearly form a basis of neighborhoods of the image of
\Lambda
as
m
goes to infinity.
∎

Remark 5.2.4
.

In Lemma
5.2.3
, one can always take
U_{0}=\gamma(\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})
, and this choice is moreover functorial so that we could have simply defined
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}
as the colimit in Lemma
5.2.3
for this fixed choice of
U_{0}
. However, to establish good functional analytic properties of
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
, it is convenient to use the more general definition; in particular, it will be convenient to take
U_{0}
to be an affinoid ball containing
\gamma(\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})
so that one obtains a colimit of Banach modules under injective transition maps.

Lemma 5.2.5
.

For any map of small v-stacks
f:Y^{\prime}\rightarrow Y
, the natural map

f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{f^{*}\Lambda/Y^{\prime}}^{f^{*}\gamma-\mathrm{la}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The presentation of Lemma
5.2.3
is compatible with pullback, and then we can use Corollary
3.1.4
.
∎

5.2.6.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum. Note that for an affinoid perfectoid
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\to Y
, the basechange
U_{0,m,Y^{\prime}}
is the diamond associated with a smooth commutative group in adic spaces, with Lie algebra
V_{Y^{\prime}}
. Then
V_{Y^{\prime}}
acts by invariant derivations on each term of the direct system, and thus on the colimit
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
. This action is moreover compatible with pullback, and thus gives an action of
V
on
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
. In other words, we have constructed a functorial
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-linear map

V\rightarrow\End_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}).

Lemma 5.2.7
.

Let
(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be as before and let
W=\ker\gamma
. Then the natural map

(5.2.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}

induced by
(\Lambda,\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y},\operatorname{id}_{\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}})\to(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
is a monomorphism and identifies
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
with the
W
-fixed points of
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}
under the action of §
5.2.6
.

Proof.

By the compatibility with pullback of Lemma
5.2.5
we may assume
Y
is an affinoid perfectoid
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
and
\Lambda
,
V
, and
W
are all trivial. Let
U^{\prime}_{0}=\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+}
and
U_{0}=\gamma(\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})
. Using the presentation of Lemma
5.2.3
twice, we can identify the natural map of the lemma with

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{m}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,m}/Y}\to\varinjlim_{m}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,m}^{\prime}/Y}.

By the discussion in §
5.2.6
, this natural map is
\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-equivariant for the action on the left hand side given by
\gamma:\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to V
. In particular, it induces a natural map

(5.2.4)

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{m}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,m}/Y}\to\left(\varinjlim_{m}\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,m}^{\prime}/Y}\right)^{W},

which we want to show is an isomorphism. Taking fixed points for the action of
W
is a finite limit (since
W
is a finitely generated free
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module) and thus passes through the filtered colimit. Thus it suffices to show that the natural map

(5.2.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,m}/Y}\to(\mathcal{O}_{U_{0,m}^{\prime}/Y})^{W}

is an isomorphism. In other words, we are trying to show that functions on a ball inside
\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
that are killed by the derivations coming from
W=\ker\gamma
,
must factor through
\gamma:\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to V
. Writing
\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}
as
W\oplus V
, see Lemma
4.3.2
, this can be checked in coordinates where it is straightforward.
∎

It is immediate from Definition
5.2.1
that there is a natural restriction map

(5.2.6)

\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}.

Lemma 5.2.8
.

The restriction map (
5.2.6
) is injective.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2.7
, it suffices to prove that the restriction map
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}
is injective. Evaluating on an affinoid perfectoid
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})/Y
where
\Lambda\simeq\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}}
is trivial, the restriction map is the natural injection
C^{\mathrm{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h},A)\hookrightarrow C^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h},A).

We conclude as such affinoid perfectoids form a basis for
Y_{\mathrm{v}}
.
∎

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
.

Proposition 5.2.9
.

Assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and that
V
is free. The following hold:

(1)

For all morphisms of fiercely v-complete Huber pairs
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
, the natural map (
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+})
)

(5.2.7)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})\otimes_{\underline{A}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})

is an isomorphism.

(2)

The natural map

(5.2.8)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)/Y}^{(\gamma\oplus\gamma)-\mathrm{la}})

is an isomorphism.

(3)

The natural map

(5.2.9)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{\underline{A}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}),\underline{R})

is an isomorphism.

(4)

The natural map

(5.2.10)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)/Y}^{(\gamma\oplus\gamma)-\mathrm{la}})

is an isomorphism.

(5)

The module
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})
is strongly countably Fréchet of compact type.

For an arbitrary small v-stack
Y
, the following hold:

(6)

The sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
is reflexive.

(7)

The sheaf
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
is locally strongly countably Fréchet of compact type.

(8)

The natural map

(5.2.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\to\mathcal{O}_{(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda)/Y}^{(\gamma\oplus\gamma)-\mathrm{la}}

is an isomorphism.

(9)

The natural map

(5.2.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\to\left(\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\right)^{\ast}

is an isomorphism.

We start by proving a lemma.

Lemma 5.2.10
.

Let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
for a fiercely v-complete Huber pair
(R,R^{+})
over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\oplus d},\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum such that
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\gamma\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\Lambda}}
factors through the unit ball
(\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y})^{d}
. For any
n\geq 0
, let
U_{n}\subseteq\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda\times\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\oplus d}
be the open subset of pairs
(\lambda,w)
where
\gamma(\lambda)-w\in p^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})^{d}
. Then

\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(Y)=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{O}(U_{n})=\varinjlim_{n}H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}),

and
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})
is a direct summand of an orthonormalizable Banach
R
-module.

Moreover, suppose
(R,R^{+})\to(B,B^{+})
is a map of fiercely v-complete Huber pairs, and let
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(B,B^{+})
. Then the natural map

(5.2.13)

H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The first equality follows from Lemma
5.2.3
.

For the rest, we first choose a perfectoid cover
Y_{\infty}=\operatorname{Spa}(R_{\infty},R^{+}_{\infty})
with
R_{\infty}=(\varinjlim_{i}R_{i})^{\wedge}
where each
R\rightarrow R_{i}
is faithfully flat finite étale and such that
\Lambda
is trivial after pullback to
Y_{\infty}
— this is possible by first taking a cover as in
[
34
, Lemma 3.6.26]
and then taking fiber products with the trivializing covers for
\Lambda/p^{i}\Lambda
. We write
R_{i}^{+}
for the integral closure of
R^{+}
in
R_{i}
, and
Y_{i}=\operatorname{Spa}(R_{i},R_{i}^{+})
.

We are going to first show that, for some index
i
,
U_{n}\times_{Y}{Y_{i}}
is a finite disjoint union of balls over
Y_{i}
. First we fix a section
\lambda:Y_{\infty}\rightarrow\Lambda_{Y_{\infty}}
. We write
\overline{\lambda}:Y_{\infty}\rightarrow(\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda)_{Y_{\infty}}
for the induced map. Then the map
p^{n}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y_{\infty}}\rightarrow\overline{\lambda}^{*}U_{n,Y_{\infty}}
,
v\mapsto\gamma(\lambda)+v
is an isomorphism, i.e.,
\overline{\lambda}^{*}U_{n,Y_{\infty}}
is a closed ball of radius
|p^{n}|
in
\mathbb{A}^{d}_{Y_{\infty}}
centered at
\gamma(\lambda)
. Writing
\gamma(\lambda)=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})\in R_{\infty}^{d}
, we find that, by the density of
\varinjlim_{i}R_{i}
in
R_{\infty}
, for
i
sufficiently large there is a center
(b_{1},\ldots,b_{d})\in R_{i}^{d}
for the same ball.

In particular, if we first pass to
i
large enough to fix an isomorphism
\Lambda/p^{n}\Lambda=(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{h}
, then we may pass to a further
i
so that for each
\alpha\in(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{h}
, we have
U_{n}|_{\alpha\times Y_{i}}
is a ball of radius
p^{n}
. Thus
U_{n}\times_{Y}{Y_{i}}
is a disjoint union of
p^{nh}

d
-dimensional balls of radius
|p^{n}|
over
Y_{i}
. It follows, in particular, that
\mathcal{O}(U_{n}\times_{Y}Y_{i})
is an orthonormalizable Banach
R_{i}
-module.

Now, since
R\rightarrow R_{i}
is faithfully flat finite étale, descent is effective along
R\rightarrow R_{i}
, and thus we find

\mathcal{O}(U_{n})\otimes_{R}R_{i}=\mathcal{O}(U_{n}\times_{Y}Y_{i}).

(in making the descent argument, note that
R_{i}/R
is finite so the completed tensor product with
R_{i}
agrees with the regular tensor product). Note that
R_{i}
is projective over
R
, so
R
is a direct summand of
R_{i}
as an
R
-module and
R_{i}
is a direct summand of
R^{m}
as an
R
-module (see the proof of Lemma
3.3.2
). It follows from Lemma
3.1.6
that
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y})
is a direct summand of an orthonormalizable Banach
R
-module.

Now suppose given a map
(R,R^{+})\rightarrow(B,B^{+})
of fiercely
v
-complete Huber pairs, let
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})
, and, for
i
as above, let
Y_{i}^{\prime}=Y_{i}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spa}(B_{i},B_{i}^{+})
. Then, since
U_{n}\times_{Y}{Y_{i}}
is a union of
p^{nh}

d
-dimensional balls of radius
|p^{n}|
, we see that the natural map

(5.2.14)

H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U_{n}\times_{Y}Y_{i},\mathcal{O})\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R_{i}}}\underline{B_{i}}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U_{n}\times_{Y}Y_{i}^{\prime},\mathcal{O})

is an isomorphism. Indeed, this amounts to checking
\underline{R_{i}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}}\rangle\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\underline{R_{i}}}\underline{B_{i}}\simeq\underline{B_{i}\langle T_{1},\ldots T_{d}\rangle}
, which
is Proposition
2.2.7
. But now applying the projectors for the direct summands as above, we conclude

H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U_{n}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime},\mathcal{O})=H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(U_{n},\mathcal{O})\otimes_{\underline{R}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{B},

giving the claimed result.
∎

In the proof of Proposition
5.2.9
, we will need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2.11
.

With the notation as in Lemma
5.2.10
, let
U_{n}^{\circ}\subset\Lambda/p^{n+1}\Lambda\times\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{\oplus d}
denote the open subset of pairs
(\lambda,w)
where
\gamma(\lambda)-w\in p^{n+\varepsilon}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{+})^{d}
for some
\varepsilon>0
. Then we have

(5.2.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(Y)=\varinjlim_{n}H^{0}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n}/Y}),

where
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n}/Y}:=\mathcal{O}^{+}_{U^{\circ}_{n}/Y}[\tfrac{1}{p}]
, and each term in the colimit is a direct summand of a countably free Smith
R
-algebra.

Proof.

This follows as in the proof of Lemma
5.2.10
, noting that
U_{n,Y_{i}}^{\circ}
is union of
p^{(n+1)h}
“open balls” of radius
|p^{n}|
, and that the restriction maps
\mathcal{O}_{U_{n}/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{U_{n+1}/Y}
factor through
\mathcal{O}_{U^{\circ}_{n}}^{\mathrm{bdd}}
. We now observe that each term in the colimit is a direct summand of a countably free Smith
R
-algebra because
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n,Y_{i}}}
is countably free Smith; indeed, one has
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n,Y_{i}}}(R,R^{+})\simeq\bigoplus_{\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{h}}R^{+}[\![T_{1},\dots,T_{d}]\!][\tfrac{1}{p}]
for any affinoid open
\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})\to Y_{i}
, see Lemma
3.3.9
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2.9
.

Part (1) follows from Lemma
5.2.10
by commuting the colimit with the solid tensor product. Part (3) follows directly from the first property and Lemma
3.2.3
. Part (2) follows directly from Lemma
5.2.10
and Lemma
3.3.4
, together with the fact that the solid tensor product commutes with direct limits.

We now continue with the notation of Lemma
5.2.11
.

Claim 5.2.12
.

The dual transition maps

(5.2.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ})^{\ast}\to\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n+1}^{\circ})^{\ast}

are compact in the sense of Section
2.3.2
and have dense image.

Proof of Claim.

We can choose an
i
sufficiently large as in the proof of Lemma
5.2.11
so that
U_{n+1}^{\circ}\times Y_{Y_{i}}
is a union of
p^{(n+2)h}
open balls of radius
|p^{(n+1)}|
,
U_{n}^{\circ}\times_{Y}{Y_{i}}
is a union of
p^{(n+1)h}
open balls of radius
|p^{n}|
, and the map
U_{n}^{\circ}\times_{Y}{Y_{i}}\rightarrow U_{n+1}^{\circ}\times_{Y}{Y_{i}}
is described on each open ball of radius
|p^{n}|
as the sum of the restriction maps to
p^{h}
(not necessarily distinct) open sub-balls of radius
|p^{n+1}|
. We will reduce to analyzing these simple restriction maps.

To that end, we first note that for
e
an
R=\mathcal{O}(Y)
-linear projector on
\mathcal{O}(Y_{i})
such that
e\mathcal{O}(Y_{i})=\mathcal{O}(Y)
, we have

e\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{\bullet}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i})=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{\bullet}^{\circ})\textrm{ for }\bullet=n,n+1.

This implies

e\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{\bullet}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i})^{*}=\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{\bullet}^{\circ})^{*}\textrm{ for }\bullet=n,n+1.

so it suffices to prove that

(5.2.17)

\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n+1}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i})^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i})^{*}

has dense image and is compact, since these properties are preserved after applying
e
.

To that end, we first note that for any of the single restriction maps from a ball of radius
|p^{n}|
to
|p^{n+1}|
, we are free to choose coordinates such that both balls are centered at zero and rescale to obtain, at the level of functions, the map

R^{+}_{i}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}]][\tfrac{1}{p}]\xrightarrow{T_{i}\mapsto pT_{i}}R^{+}_{i}[[T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}]][\tfrac{1}{p}].

The dual of this map is

(5.2.18)

R_{i}^{*}\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle\xrightarrow{S_{i}\mapsto pS_{i}}R_{i}^{*}\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle

where
R_{i}^{*}
denotes the
R
-linear dual. Since
R_{i}
is projective, so is
R_{i}^{*}
, thus for some
N
this is a direct summand of the map

(R\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle\xrightarrow{S_{i}\mapsto pS_{i}}R\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle)^{\oplus N}.

Thus, to see (
5.2.18
) has dense image and is compact, it suffices to observe that

(5.2.19)

R\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle\xrightarrow{S_{i}\mapsto pS_{i}}R\langle S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle.

has dense image and is compact (since these properties are preserved under finite direct sums and, by applying a projector as earlier in the proof, direct summands). The image of (
5.2.19
) is dense because it contains the polynomials
R[S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}]
, and we have already argued at the end of the proof of Lemma
5.1.2
that (
5.2.19
) is compact.

Since (
5.2.18
) has dense image, so does (
5.2.17
): this follows already by considering a single ball in
U_{n+1}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i}
contained in each ball in
U_{n}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i}
. Similarly, by grouping the restriction maps according to their source ball in
U_{n}^{\circ}\times_{Y}Y_{i}
, we find that (
5.2.18
) is a direct sum of
p^{nh}
maps, each of which is a sum of
p^{h}
compact maps, and thus it is compact.
∎

The claim proves part (5). Now part (4) follows from (2) together with part (3) and Corollary
2.4.11
.

Parts (6)-(9) are v-local on the base and so we may assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+})
with
(A,A^{+})
perfectoid and with
\Lambda
and
V
free. We prove (6) directly, by computing

(5.2.20)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n}},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

(5.2.21)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\varprojlim_{n}\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n}},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

(5.2.22)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U^{\circ}_{n}},\mathcal{O}_{Y}),\mathcal{O}_{Y})

as in the proof of Corollary
3.2.11
, using (5). We conclude by the reflexivity of (direct summands of) countably free smith
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules, see Proposition
3.2.2
. Part (7) follows by applying part (5) to affinoid perfectoid
(A,A^{+})
over
Y
and similarly part (8) follows from part (2) and part (9) follows from parts (2)-(4).
∎

5.2.13.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
.

Lemma 5.2.14
.

Both
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
are naturally solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras.

Proof.

For
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
, the algebra structure is the natural one (and the multiplication structure factors through the solid tensor product), induced from the individual algebra structures on the terms in the colimit defining
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
. For the co-algebra structure, consider the morphism

\Lambda\oplus\Lambda\xrightarrow{\gamma\oplus\gamma}V\oplus V.

The co-algebra structure is now induced from pulling back along the addition map
\Lambda\oplus\Lambda\to\Lambda
and using Proposition
5.2.9
-(8). The co-unit is induced from the natural map
0\to\Lambda
.

This solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
induces a solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
once we show that the natural map

(5.2.23)

\left(\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}\right)^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}\otimes^{\blacksquare}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}

is an isomorphism. But this is part (9) of Proposition
5.2.9
.
∎

6.
The Fourier transform

In this section we construct the Fourier transform for
p
-divisible v-groups over a small v-stack and establish the main results of this article. In §
6.1
, we construct the universal character
\kappa
and establish that it is analytic (Lemma
6.1.12
). In §
6.2
, we use this to define the Fourier transform. In §
6.3
, we prove Theorem
2
(see Theorem
6.3.1
), and in §
6.4
, we prove Proposition
1.1.2
. Finally, we prove Theorem
1
in §
6.5
, and in §
6.6
, we compare our results with those of Schneider–Teitelbaum
[
41
]
.

6.1.
The universal character

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\Lambda
be a locally free sheaf of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
-modules on
Y_{\mathrm{v}}
. Let
\Lambda^{\ast}:=\Hom_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(\Lambda,\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
denote its linear dual. Recall that:

•

\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}
denotes the group over
Y
satisfying
\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}(R,R^{+})=R
for any
(R,R^{+})\in Y_{\mathrm{v}}
;

•

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}
denotes the group over
Y
satisfying
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}(R,R^{+})=1+R^{00}
for any
(R,R^{+})\in Y_{\mathrm{v}}
, where
R^{00}
denotes the ideal of topologically nilpotent elements.

Note that all of these sheaves of abelian groups come equipped with a natural action of
\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, see §
4.1.2
.

6.1.1.

Recall from Example
4.1.4
that there is a logarithm map
\operatorname{log}^{\lozenge}\colon\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}
. Recall moreover that, for
\varpi=2p
, the map
\operatorname{log}^{\lozenge}
is an isomorphism from
\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|)
to
\mathbb{B}(0,|\varpi|)
, with inverse defined by the usual exponential series.

6.1.2.

We let
H_{\Lambda}=\Lambda^{*}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}
be the full character group of
\Lambda
(in terms of Definition
4.3.6
, this is the group
H_{\mathcal{X}}
corresponding to the locally analytic character datum
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y},\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}})
).

Definition 6.1.3
.

Let
i\geq 0
and set
\varpi=2p
. We define
H^{(i)}_{\Lambda}\subset H_{\Lambda}
to be the open
13
13

13

To see that this is open, we can check this v-locally – in particular, we may assume that
\Lambda
is free, and then the result is clear.
subgroup defined by

H^{(i)}_{\Lambda}=\Lambda^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|^{1/p^{i}})_{Y}^{\lozenge}

where
\mathbb{B}(1,|\varpi|^{1/p^{i}})\subset\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
denotes the subset defined by the inequality
|T|^{p^{i}}\leq|\varpi|
.

Note that
H_{\Lambda}=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}H^{(i)}_{\Lambda}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}/Y}
is locally strongly countably Fréchet, with a presentation given by
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}/Y}=\varprojlim_{i}\mathcal{O}_{H^{(i)}_{\Lambda}/Y}
, see Proposition
5.1.1
-(3).

6.1.4.

Let
Z_{\Lambda}=\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}
. We will also consider certain open subgroups of this space thickening
\Lambda
as in Lemma
5.2.10
. Explicitly:

Definition 6.1.5
.

For
i\geq 0
, consider the open affinoid subgroup
\mathbb{G}^{(n)}_{a}\subset\mathbb{G}_{a}
given by
\mathbb{G}^{(n)}_{a}(R,R^{+})=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(R)+p^{i}R^{+}
. We let

Z_{\Lambda,i}:=\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{(i),\lozenge}\subset Z_{\Lambda}

denote the corresponding open subgroup.

Lemma 6.1.6
.

\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\Lambda,i}/Y}
is locally countably orthonormalizable Banach.

Proof.

For
(R,R^{+})\in Y_{\mathrm{v}}
such that
\Lambda|_{\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})}
is free of rank
h
, if we fix an isomorphism
\Lambda|_{\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})}\cong\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}}
, then
Z_{\Lambda,n,\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})}
is a disjoint union of balls indexed by
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{h}
. The lemma now follows from Lemma
3.3.1
.
∎

For
i\geq 0
, also consider the subgroup
Z_{\Lambda,i}^{\circ}\subset Z_{\Lambda,i}
given by the sheafification of

(R,R^{+})\mapsto p^{i}\left(\Lambda(R)\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(R)}R^{+}\right).

Lemma 6.1.7
.

The natural map
\Lambda\oplus Z_{\Lambda,i}^{\circ}\rightarrow Z_{\Lambda,i},\;(\lambda,z)\mapsto\lambda+z
is a surjection with kernel
p^{i}\Lambda
embedded antidiagonally by
\lambda\mapsto(\lambda,-\lambda)
.

Proof.

Immediate.
∎

Consider the natural pairing

\displaystyle\kappa_{\Lambda}:\Lambda\times H_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}

\displaystyle(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\otimes z)

\displaystyle\mapsto z^{\lambda^{\prime}(\lambda)}.

It is bilinear:

(6.1.1)

\kappa(x_{1}+x_{2},y)=\kappa(x_{1},y)\kappa(x_{2},y)\textrm{ and }\kappa(x,y_{1}y_{2})=\kappa(x,y_{1})\kappa(x,y_{2}).

Since
\mathbb{G}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1,\lozenge}_{Y}
, we may view
\kappa_{\Lambda}
as an element of
\mathcal{O}(\Lambda\times_{Y}H_{\Lambda})=\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}(H_{\Lambda}).

Lemma 6.1.8
.

For each
i\geq 0
, the restriction
\kappa|_{\Lambda\times H^{(i)}_{\Lambda}}
extends naturally to a pairing

\kappa_{\Lambda,i}:Z_{\Lambda,i}\times H_{\Lambda}^{(i)}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}.

In particular,

\kappa_{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{\Lambda})\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}(H_{\Lambda})
.

Proof.

We can define a pairing on
Z_{\Lambda,i}^{\circ}\times H_{\Lambda}^{(i)}

by

(\lambda\otimes r,\lambda^{\prime}\otimes z)\mapsto\mathrm{exp}(\lambda^{\prime}(\lambda)r\log(z)),

where the exponential converges because
\lambda^{\prime}(\lambda)\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}^{+}
,
r\in p^{i}\mathcal{O}^{+}
, and
\log(z)\in p^{-i}\varpi\mathcal{O}^{+}
, so that the product lies in
\varpi\mathcal{O}^{+}
. This pairing agrees with
\kappa_{\Lambda}
on
p^{i}\Lambda\times H^{(i)}_{\Lambda}
, thus we obtain the desired pairing
\kappa_{\Lambda,i}
by invoking Lemma
6.1.7
. Since

\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{\Lambda})=\varprojlim_{i}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{\Lambda}^{(i)})=\varprojlim_{i}\varinjlim_{j}\mathcal{O}(Z_{\Lambda,j}\times H_{\Lambda}^{(i)})

we conclude
\kappa_{\Lambda}\in\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{\Lambda})
(note that for
j\geq i
, we have
\kappa_{\Lambda,j}|_{Z_{\Lambda,j}\times_{Y}H_{\Lambda}^{(i)}}=\kappa_{\Lambda,i}|_{Z_{\lambda,j}\times_{Y}H_{\Lambda}^{(i)}}
).
∎

Example 6.1.9
.

Suppose that
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
for
(R,R^{+})
a fiercely v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and let
\Lambda=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
be the trivial local system. Then
H_{\Lambda}(Y)=\mathbb{G}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge}(Y)=1+R^{00}
and
Z_{\Lambda,i}(Y)=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(R)+p^{i}R^{+}
. The pairing
\kappa
is given by

\kappa(z,1+t)=(1+t)^{z}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{z\choose k}t^{k}\;\in\;1+R^{00}.

For
z\in 1+p^{i}R^{+}
, we find this series converges for
t\in\varpi^{1/p^{i}}R^{+}
. This can also be deduced from Amice’s description of an orthonormal basis for
\mathcal{O}(Z_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},n})
(see §
A.2
).

Lemma 6.1.10
.

(1)

For the derivation action
\cdot
of
\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}/H_{\Lambda}}^{\mathrm{la}}
,

\lambda\cdot\kappa_{\Lambda}=\langle\lambda,\log_{H_{\Lambda}}\rangle\kappa_{\Lambda},

where we view
\log_{H_{\Lambda}}
as a section of
\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}^{*}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}}
over
H_{\Lambda}
.

(2)

For the derivation action
\cdot
of
\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}^{*}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}}
on
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}/H_{\Lambda}}
,

\lambda^{\prime}\cdot\kappa_{\lambda}=\lambda^{\prime}\kappa_{\Lambda},

where on the right-hand side
\lambda^{\prime}
is viewed as a function on
\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}
. Here we are identifying
\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}^{*}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}}=\operatorname{Lie}H_{\Lambda}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}}
.

Proof.

For both statements, it suffices to work over an affinoid perfectoid
Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\rightarrow H_{\Lambda}
such that
\Lambda|_{Y^{\prime}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}
is trivial. Then, fixing such a trivialization, we may view
\kappa_{\Lambda}
as an element of
C^{\mathrm{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h},A)
and view
\log_{H_{\Lambda}}
as an element of
(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h})^{*}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}A
. By construction, for
x
in a small enough neighborhood of
0\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h}
,
\kappa(x)=\exp(\langle x,\log_{H_{\Lambda}}\rangle).

Let
A[\varepsilon]
denote the ring of dual numbers over
A
(so
\varepsilon^{2}=0
). For
\lambda\in(\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}})(Y^{\prime})=A^{h}
, we can then compute

\displaystyle(\lambda\cdot\kappa_{\Lambda})(x)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}\cdot(\kappa_{\Lambda}(x+\varepsilon\lambda)-\kappa_{\Lambda}(x))

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\Lambda}(x)\varepsilon^{-1}(\kappa_{\Lambda}(\varepsilon\lambda)-1)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\Lambda}(x)\varepsilon^{-1}\exp(\langle\varepsilon\lambda,\log_{H_{\Lambda}}\rangle-1)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\Lambda}(x)\langle\lambda,\log_{H_{\Lambda}}\rangle.

This establishes (1). Similarly, we obtain (2) by computing

\displaystyle(\lambda^{\prime}\cdot\kappa_{\Lambda})(x)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}(\exp(\lambda^{\prime}\varepsilon)\kappa_{\Lambda}(x))-\kappa_{\Lambda}(x))

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}((1+\lambda^{\prime}\varepsilon)\kappa_{\Lambda}(x)-\kappa_{\Lambda}(x))

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\prime}\kappa_{\Lambda}(x).

∎

6.1.11.
General locally analytic character groups

Let
Y
be a small v-stack, and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum as in Definition
4.3.6
with associated character group
H_{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq H_{\Lambda}
. We let
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}
be the restriction of
\kappa_{\Lambda}
to
\Lambda\times_{Y}H_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Lemma 6.1.12
.

One has
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(H_{\mathcal{X}})
. Furthermore, this section is contravariantly functorial in the character datum
\mathcal{X}
in the following sense: If
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=(\Lambda^{\prime},V^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
is another locally analytic character datum and we have a morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, then the images of
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
agree under the natural maps
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(H_{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(H_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}})
and
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma^{\prime}-\mathrm{la}}(H_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}})\to\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda^{\prime}/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(H_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}})
respectively.

Proof.

The functoriality is immediate from the definitions, so we will just verify that
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(H_{\mathcal{X}})
.
It is equivalent to verify that
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}
, viewed as a global section of
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}/H_{\mathcal{X}}}^{\mathrm{la}}
over
H_{\mathcal{X}}
, lies in
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}/H_{\mathcal{X}}}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
. By Lemma
5.2.7
, for
W:=\ker\gamma
, it is then equivalent to verify that
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}
is annihilated by the invariant derivation action of
W\subseteq\Lambda_{H_{\Lambda}}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}}.
But by Lemma
6.1.10
, a section
w
of
W
acts as multiplication by the function
\langle w,\log_{H_{\Lambda}}|_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}\rangle
. Since, by the definition of
H_{\mathcal{X}}
, we have that
\log_{H_{\Lambda}}|_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}=\log_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}
lies in the annihilator of
W
, we conclude.
∎

6.2.
Constructing the Fourier transforms

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
as in Definition
4.3.6
. Recall that

\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}:=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}},\mathcal{O}_{Y})\textrm{ and }\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y},\mathcal{O}_{Y}).

We now use the universal character
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}
to define our Fourier transforms.

6.2.1.

For
\pi:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow Y
, we define
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
to be the composition of the natural maps

\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}/H_{\mathcal{X}}}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\xrightarrow{\mu\mapsto\mu(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}=\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}.

From the functoriality properties of Lemma
6.1.12
, it follows that
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a natural transformation between covariant functors of the character datum
\mathcal{X}
.

6.2.2.

For
\rho:\Lambda\rightarrow Y
, we define
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
to be the composition of the natural maps (where now we consider
\kappa
as an element of
\mathcal{O}_{H\times_{Y}\Lambda/\Lambda}(\Lambda)
)

\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}\rightarrow\rho_{*}\rho^{*}\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}=\rho_{*}\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}\times_{Y}\Lambda/\Lambda}\xrightarrow{\mu\mapsto\mu(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}=\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}.

Lemma 6.2.3
.

With notation as above,
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
factors through a unique map

\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}

via the inclusion
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}
.

Proof.

Uniqueness follows from the injectivity of
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}
(Lemma
5.2.8
). Thus, to see that the factorization exists, it suffices to verify it assuming
Y
is affinoid perfectoid and
V
is free. We then take subgroups
H_{n}
as in Lemma
5.1.2
. Applying Lemma
3.2.10
, we find

\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}=\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{D}_{H_{n}/Y},

so it suffices to treat the analogous maps

\mathcal{D}_{H_{n}/Y}\rightarrow\rho_{*}\rho^{*}\mathcal{D}_{H_{n}/Y}=\rho_{*}\mathcal{D}_{H_{n}\times_{Y}\Lambda/\Lambda}\xrightarrow{\mu\mapsto\mu(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}|_{H_{n}\times_{Y}\Lambda})}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}=\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}.

Restricting to any affinoid perfectoid
Y^{\prime}/Y
where
\Lambda
is free of finite rank and
W=\ker\gamma
is free, Lemma
6.1.12
implies

{\left.\kern-1.2pt\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{Y^{\prime}}}\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h},\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}/Y}(Y^{\prime}))^{W=0}\subseteq C^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h},\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}/Y}(Y^{\prime}))=\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}\times_{Y}\Lambda/\Lambda}(Y^{\prime}_{\Lambda}).

Applying a section
\mu
of
\mathcal{D}_{H_{n}/Y}(Y^{\prime})
is accomplished on the coefficients, so we find that the image of such a
\mu
lies in

C^{\mathrm{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{h},\mathcal{O}(Y^{\prime}))^{W=0}

and thus is
\gamma
-locally analytic by Lemma
5.2.7
.
∎

From the functoriality properties of Lemma
6.1.12
, it follows that
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
is a natural transformation of contravariant functors on character data
\mathcal{X}
.

6.2.4.

Recall from §
5.1.4
and Lemma
5.2.14
that all of the solid sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-modules
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
,
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
,
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
, are naturally solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras.

Lemma 6.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum.

(1)

The Fourier transforms
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
are morphisms of solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras.

(2)

The Fourier transforms
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
are mutually dual under the reflexivity of
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
proved in Proposition
5.1.1
-(2) and Proposition
5.2.9
-(2).

Proof.

The first part follows from functoriality in the character datum. For example, consider the diagonal morphism

\Delta\colon\Lambda\to\Lambda\oplus\Lambda

which induces a morphism of character data
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}\oplus\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda\oplus\Lambda,V\oplus V,\gamma\oplus\gamma)
. The linear dual of this map
\Delta^{\ast}\colon\Lambda^{\ast}\oplus\Lambda^{\ast}\to\Lambda^{\ast}
is the addition map, and induces the multiplication map
H_{\mathcal{X}}\times H_{\mathcal{X}}\to H_{\mathcal{X}}
via the identification
H_{\mathcal{X}}\times_{Y}H_{\mathcal{X}}=H_{\mathcal{X}\oplus\mathcal{X}}
. On the other hand, the diagonal map induces the diagonal
Z_{\Lambda,n}\to Z_{\Lambda\oplus\Lambda,n}=Z_{\Lambda,n}\times Z_{\Lambda,n}
, which in turn induces the algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{Z_{\Lambda,n}/Y}
(and hence
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\operatorname{-la}}
). The functoriality of
\mathbb{F}^{\bullet}
with respect to the diagonal map
\Delta
now implies that
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
is a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras. The other assertions about
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
and the comultiplication structures can be proved similarly, and we leave the details to the reader.

We now establish the second part. Let
\rho:\Lambda\rightarrow Y
and
\pi:H_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow Y
denote the structure maps. We claim that for
Y^{\prime}/Y
,
\mu\in\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(Y^{\prime})
, and
\delta\in\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}(Y^{\prime})
,

(6.2.1)

\delta((\pi^{*}\mu)(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}))=\mu((\rho^{*}\delta)(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})).

Indeed, as in the proof of Lemma
6.2.3
, it suffices to assume
Y=Y^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
is affinoid perfectoid with
V
trivial, and to replace
H_{\mathcal{X}}
with one of the
H_{n}
’s as in Lemma
5.1.2
. Then, for
U_{i}
as in Lemma
5.2.10
,

\displaystyle\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda\times_{Y}H_{n}/H_{n}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{*}\varinjlim_{i}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}\times_{Y}H_{n}/H_{n}}

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{i}\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}\times_{Y}H_{n}/H_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{i}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}\times_{Y}H_{n}/Y}\right)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{i}\left(\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}/Y}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}/Y}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\varinjlim_{i}\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}/Y}\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}/Y}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}/Y}

where the second equality is because
\pi:H_{n}\rightarrow Y
is quasi-compact, the fourth equality is by Lemma
3.3.5
, and the fifth equality is because colimits and solid tensor products commute.
In this presentation, (
6.2.1
) can be unwound to the statement that we can concatenate
\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\Lambda\times_{Y}H_{n}}
, viewed as a section of this sheaf, with
\mu
and
\delta
in either order.

Given (
6.2.1
), the verification that
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
are mutually dual is essentially formal: for example, we have

(\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\delta)(\mu)=\delta(\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mu))=\delta(\pi^{*}\mu(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}))=\mu(\rho^{*}\delta(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}))=\mu(\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}(\delta)).

∎

Lemma 6.2.6
.

If
Y=\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathcal{X}=(\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},\mathcal{O}_{Y},\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\xrightarrow{}\mathcal{O}_{Y})
, then
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For
(A,A^{+})
affinoid perfectoid over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, the natural map

(6.2.2)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}/Y})\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{A}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}/Y})

is an isomorphism by Proposition
5.1.1
-(1). Furthermore, the natural map

(6.2.3)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}/Y}^{\mathrm{la}})\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}^{\blacksquare}\underline{A}\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(\operatorname{Spd}(A,A^{+}),\mathcal{D}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}/Y}^{\mathrm{la}})

is an isomorphism by Proposition
5.2.9
-(1),(3) and Corollary
2.4.12
. Thus it suffices to show that
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
induces an isomorphism on condensed global sections.

It follows from Proposition
5.2.9
-(3) that

(6.2.4)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}/Y}^{\mathrm{la}})=\Hom_{\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}/Y}),\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}),

and from Example
6.1.9
that we may identify
(\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}})(\ast)
with the bijection
\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}}
of Corollary
A.2.6
. We conclude
14
14

14

They are Fréchet spaces by Proposition
A.1.4
together with Corollary
5.1.3
and Proposition
5.2.9
-(5).
that
F_{\mathcal{X}}(\ast)_{\mathrm{top}}
is a continuous bijection between two Fréchet spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and thus an isomorphism by the open mapping theorem for
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-Frechet spaces (see
[
42
, Proposition 8.6]
). It follows from Proposition
A.1.4
that
{\mathbb{F}}_{\mathcal{X}}
itself is an isomorphism.
∎

6.3.
The Fourier transforms are isomorphisms

The main theorem of this article is the following (it is Theorem
2
from the introduction). Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
.

Theorem 6.3.1
.

The Fourier transforms
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
and

\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
are isomorphisms of solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras.

By Lemma
6.2.5
-(1), it suffices to prove that
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
is an isomorphism, and this is the main goal of the rest of §
6.3
.

Proposition 6.3.2
.

Let
Y
be a small v-stack and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
. Let
W=\ker\gamma
.

(1)

The natural map
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
identifies the target with the quotient of the source by the action of
W
given by precomposing with multiplication by
\langle w,\operatorname{log}_{H_{\Lambda}}\rangle
.

(2)

The natural map
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}\to\mathcal{D}_{H_{\Lambda}/Y}

identifies the source with the kernel of the dual
W
-action on the target.

Proof of Proposition
6.3.2
.

The second claim follows from the first since
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}(-,\mathcal{O}_{Y})
turns colimits into limits. For the first claim, we may assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
such that
V
,
\Lambda
, and
W
are all trivial of constant rank. Consider the Cartesian diagram

(6.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{\ast}\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathbb{G}_{a,Y}^{\lozenge}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\log}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

The ideal sheaf
\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
cutting out
H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}}
is a pseudo-coherent sheaf in the sense of
[
35
, Section 2.5]
by
15
15

15

Note that
H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}}
and
H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}}
are smooth sousperfectoid adic spaces over
Y
, this follows from Lemma
5.1.2
together with
[
21
, Definition 7.1, Lemma 7.5, and Lemma 7.3]
.

[
13
, Proposition IV.4.19]
and is generated by functionals of the form
\langle w,-\rangle
for
w\in W(\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+}))
by
[
13
, Proposition IV.4.19]
. This shows that the natural map
\mathcal{O}(H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}})\to\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})
factors through the natural map
\mathcal{O}(H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}})\to\mathcal{O}(H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}})_{W}
and an injection
\mathcal{O}(H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}})_{W}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})
; it now suffices to show that
\mathcal{O}(H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}})\to\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})
is surjective.

The natural map
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}}}
is surjective by
[
14
, Proposition IV.4.19]
. The surjectivity on global sections follows directly from

[
35
, Theorem 2.6.5.(iii)]
since
H_{\Lambda}
is a quasi-stein space in the sense of
[
35
, Definition 2.6.2]
by Lemma
5.1.2
. We give an alternative argument below for the surjectivity on global sections.

Let
k
be the rank of
W
, and choose a filtration
W=W_{0}\supsetneq W_{1}\supsetneq\cdots\supsetneq W_{k}=0
with free graded pieces, using the freeness of
W
. Set
V_{i}=\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}/W_{i}
, let
\gamma_{i}
be the natural map
\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to V_{i}
and let
\mathcal{X}_{i}=(\Lambda,V_{i},\gamma_{i})
. There is a filtration

(6.3.2)

\displaystyle H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}}

\displaystyle=H_{0}\subset H_{1}\subset\cdots\subset H_{k}=H_{\Lambda}^{\mathrm{rig}},

where
H_{i}=H_{\mathcal{X}_{i}}^{\mathrm{rig}}
. It remains to show that for all
i
the map

(6.3.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(H_{i})\to\mathcal{O}(H_{i-1})

is surjective. Write
\mathcal{J}_{i}\subset\mathcal{O}_{H_{i}}
for the ideal sheaf cutting out
H_{i-1}
, which is a line bundle because
H_{i-1}
has codimension one, see
[
13
, Proposition IV.4.19]
and its proof. Write
H_{i}=\cup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}H_{i}^{(n)}
as in the proof of Lemma
5.1.2
. Evaluating the short exact sequence

(6.3.4)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{J}_{i}\to\mathcal{O}_{H_{i}}\to\mathcal{O}_{H_{i-1}}\to 0

on
H_{i}^{(n)}
gives the short exact sequence (again by
[
13
, Proposition IV.4.19]
)

(6.3.5)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{J}_{i}^{(n)}\to\mathcal{O}(H_{i}^{(n)})\to\mathcal{O}(H_{i-1}^{(n)})\to 0.

We now consider the commutative diagram

(6.3.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{J}_{i}^{(n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\mathcal{J}_{i}^{(n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\mathcal{J}_{i}^{(n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{i}^{(n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{i}^{(n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{i}^{(n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{i-1}^{(n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{i-1}^{(n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{i-1}^{(n)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}

Since the transition maps in all three inverse system have dense image by Lemma
5.1.2
and the fact that
\mathcal{J}_{i}
is a line bundle, the rows are exact by the proof of Proposition
2.4.3
. The middle and right columns are exact by the exactness of countable products, and the exactness of the left column now follows from the snake lemma. Since taking global sections of a sheaf on
H_{i}
is the same as taking the inverse limit of the sections on
H_{i}^{(n)}
, we see that

(6.3.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(H_{i})\to\mathcal{O}(H_{i-1})

is surjective, concluding the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.3.1
.

By Lemma
6.2.5
, the Fourier transforms are dual morphisms of solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras, so it suffices to show that
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
is an isomorphism of abelian sheaves. The main idea is to reduce to a statement about the classical Amice transform. More precisely, the claim is local on
Y
, so we may assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
is affinoid perfectoid,
\Lambda\cong\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\oplus h}
is trivial, and
W
is free. In this setting, consider the locally analytic character datum
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=(\Lambda,\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y},\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\mathcal{O}_{Y}})
. We of course have a morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, which induces a morphism
H_{\mathcal{X}}\to H_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}=H_{\Lambda}=\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta,Y}^{\lozenge,\oplus h}
. By Proposition
6.3.2
and Lemma
5.2.7
, we see that one can obtain
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
by passing to the
W
-invariants of

\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{D}_{H_{\Lambda}/Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}.

It therefore suffices to show that
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism. Using the Künneth formulas for
\mathcal{O}^{\operatorname{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}
, see Proposition
5.2.9
-(4), and for
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\Lambda}/Y}
, see Proposition
5.1.1
-(8), we deduce that it is enough to prove the theorem for
\Lambda=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Since
\Lambda=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is base-changed from
\operatorname{Spd}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we may assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Using Lemma
6.2.5
, we can equivalently show that the dual map

(6.3.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}/Y}^{\mathrm{la}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}/Y}

is an isomorphism. and then the result is Lemma
6.2.6
. This concludes the proof.
∎

6.3.3.

We now deduce a version of Theorem
1
over a fiercely v-complete affinoid.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a fiercely v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
. Let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
such that
V
is free. In this case, the solid Hopf
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra structures on
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
,
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y},

\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
induce solid Hopf
\underline{R}
-algebra structures upon taking global sections:

•

For
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})
, this follows from Proposition
5.1.1
-(2),(7).

•

For
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})
, this follows from Proposition
5.1.1
-(4),(7),(8).

•

For
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}})
, this follows from Proposition
5.2.9
-(2),(8).

•

For
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}})
, this follows from Proposition
5.2.9
-(4),(8),(9).

Moreover, we find the solid Hopf
\underline{R}
-algebras
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})
and
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})
are mutually dual Hopf
\underline{R}
-algebras by further invoking Proposition
5.1.1
-(3), and similarly
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}})
and
H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}})
are mutually dual Hopf
\underline{R}
-algebras by Proposition
5.2.9
-(3).

Corollary 6.3.4
.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a fiercely v-complete Huber pair over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, let
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(R,R^{+})
and let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum over
Y
. If
V
is free, then the Fourier transforms

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\colon H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}})

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\colon H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}})\to H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})

are dual isomorphisms of solid
\underline{R}
-Hopf algebras.

Proof.

In light of the above discussion, this is immediate from Theorem
6.3.1
by taking global sections.
∎

Specializing Corollary
6.3.4
to
(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
for
K/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
a
p
-adic non-archimedean extension gives Theorem
1
.

Remark 6.3.5
.

We expect the computations of §
6.3.3
and thus also Corollary
6.3.4
to hold whenever
V
is an étale (equivalently, analytic) vector bundle.

6.4.
Equivariance under invariant derivations

We now establish the equivariance properties for the Fourier transforms
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
for invariant derivations generalizing those appearing in Proposition
1.1.2
.

6.4.1.

Recall from §
5.2.6
that there is a natural action of
V
on
\mathcal{O}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}
by invariant derivations. It extends to an action of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V
(by invariant differential operators; note the Lie bracket is trivial). We equip
\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}
with the dual action of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V
. These actions are evidently functorial in
\mathcal{X}
.

There is a natural map
\log_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}:H_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow V^{*}
, so
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
is naturally a
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V
-algebra by pullback along
\log_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}
of the polynomial functions
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{V^{*}/Y}
. We equip
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
with the dual action of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V
. These actions are evidently functorial in
\mathcal{X}
.

Proposition 6.4.2
.

The Fourier transforms
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
are equivariant for the above actions of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V
.

Proof.

By the duality of Lemma
6.2.5
, it suffices to treat
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
. For
v
a section of
V
and
\mu
a section of
\mathcal{D}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}
,

\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}(v\cdot\mu)=(v\cdot\mu)(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})=\mu(v\cdot\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})=\mu(\langle v,\log_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}\rangle\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})

where the final equality is deduced from Lemma
6.1.10
-(1) and functoriality in
\mathcal{X}
. This is equal to

\langle v,\log_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}\rangle\mu(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})

because, by the construction of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
, we are applying
\mu
after pulling it back to an element of
D^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}/H_{\mathcal{X}}}
, so that it is
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}}
-linear.
∎

6.4.3.

Since
\operatorname{Lie}H_{\mathcal{X}}
is identified with
V^{\ast}
, we obtain an action of
V^{\ast}
on
\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
through invariant derivations. It extends to an action of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V^{\ast}
(by invariant differential operators; note the Lie bracket is trivial). We equip
\mathcal{D}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y}
with the dual action of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V^{\ast}
. These actions are evidently functorial in
\mathcal{X}
.

There is a natural map
\gamma|_{\Lambda}:\Lambda\rightarrow V
, which induces by pullback an algebra map
\mathcal{O}_{V/Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}
. By definition, it factors through
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}
. In particular, restricting to the polynomial functions
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V^{\ast}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{V/Y}
, we obtain a
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V^{\ast}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}
. We equip
\mathcal{D}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}
with the dual action of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V^{\ast}
. These actions are evidently functorial in
\mathcal{X}
.

Proposition 6.4.4
.

The Fourier transforms
\mathbb{F}^{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
are equivariant for the actions of
\operatorname{Sym}^{\bullet}V^{\ast}
described above.

Proof.

By the duality of Lemma
6.2.5
, it suffices to treat
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}
. For
v^{\prime}
a section of
V^{*}
and
\mu
a section of
\mathcal{D}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}_{\Lambda/Y}
,

\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}(v^{\prime}\cdot\mu)=(v^{\prime}\cdot\mu)(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})=\mu((\gamma^{*}v^{\prime})\kappa_{\mathcal{X}}).

On the other hand,

v^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mu)=v^{\prime}\cdot\mu(\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})=\mu(v^{\prime}\cdot\kappa_{\mathcal{X}})

where the final equality holds because the differentiation is happening in the coefficients direction. By functoriality, the result then follows from Lemma
6.1.10
-(2).
∎

Specialising Propositions
6.4.2
and
6.4.4
to
Y=\operatorname{Spd}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
completes the proof of Proposition
1.1.2
in the introduction.

6.5.
Classical interpretation over a field

Let
K
be a non-archimedean field with completed algebraic closure
C
. We will now explain how to interpret Corollary
6.3.4
for
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
in terms of locally convex topological vector spaces over
K
.

6.5.1.

Let
\mathcal{X}=(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
be a locally analytic character datum and suppose that
V
is trivial (equivalently, an étale vector bundle). Then concretely, we can think of
\Lambda
as a continuous representation of
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
on a finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module
\Lambda
, of
V
as a finite dimensional
K
-vector space, and of
\gamma:\Lambda_{C}\twoheadrightarrow V_{C}
as a
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
-equivariant surjection. Let
\mathcal{O}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(\Lambda)
be the locally convex vector space of locally analytic functions from
\Lambda
to
C
that are
\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}/K)
-equivariant and
\gamma
-locally analytic, i.e., locally on
\Lambda
, factor as the composition of
\gamma
with an analytic function on an open ball in
V_{C}
. We let
\mathcal{D}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}(\Lambda)
be its dual equipped with the strong topology, see Section
A.1.8
.

Let
H_{\mathcal{X}}
be the
p
-divisible v-group over
\operatorname{Spd}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
associated with
\mathcal{X}
. Since
V
is an étale vector bundle, this is the diamond associated to a
p
-divisible rigid analytic group over
\operatorname{Spa}(K,\mathcal{O}_{K})
by Proposition
4.1.10
, which we will denote by
H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}}
. Let us write
\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})
for the locally convex
K
-Fréchet space of global sections.

Lemma 6.5.2
.

There are natural isomorphisms

(6.5.1)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})}

(6.5.2)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\mathcal{D}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}(\Lambda)}.

Proof.

For the second isomorphism, we first recall from §
6.3.3

that

(6.5.3)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{D}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\underline{K}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}),\underline{K}).

We now continue in the notation of Lemma
5.2.11

(6.5.4)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\underline{K}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda/Y}^{\gamma-\mathrm{la}}),\underline{K})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\underline{K}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\varinjlim_{n}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U_{n}^{\circ}/Y}),\underline{K})

(6.5.5)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\underline{K}}(\varinjlim_{n}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U_{n}^{\circ}/Y}),\underline{K})

(6.5.6)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\Hom_{\underline{K}}(H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}_{U_{n}^{\circ}/Y}),\underline{K})

(6.5.7)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\Hom_{\underline{K}}(\underline{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ})},\underline{K}),

where the last equality is Lemma
3.3.9
. We can similarly write (
\mathrm{lcv}
stands for locally convex)

(6.5.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{O}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}(\Lambda)\right)^{\prime}_{b}

(6.5.9)

\displaystyle=\left(\varinjlim_{n}^{\mathrm{lcv}}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ})\right)^{\prime}_{b}

(6.5.10)

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}\left(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ})\right)^{\prime}_{b}.

It is a consequence of Lemma
A.1.9
that

(6.5.11)

\displaystyle\underline{\left(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ})\right)^{\prime}_{b}}\simeq\Hom_{\underline{K}}(\underline{\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{bdd}}(U_{n}^{\circ})},\underline{K}).

This, together with the commutation of
W\mapsto\underline{W}
with limits, concludes the proof of the second isomorphism.

For the first isomorphism, we write (in the notation of Lemma
5.1.2
)

(6.5.12)

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{\mathcal{X}}/Y})=\varprojlim_{n}H^{0}_{\mathrm{cond}}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{H_{n}/Y})=\varprojlim_{n}\underline{\mathcal{O}(H_{n})},

using Lemma
3.1.6
. We similarly have

(6.5.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})=\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}(H_{n}),

and we conclude as before.
∎

6.5.3.

We have the following corollary of Theorem
1
.

Corollary 6.5.4
.

There is an isomorphism of locally convex
K
-vector spaces

(6.5.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})

functorial in
(\Lambda,V,\gamma)
.

Proof.

Lemma
6.5.2
and Theorem
1
give an isomorphism

\underline{\mathcal{D}^{\gamma\mathrm{-la}}(\Lambda)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\mathcal{O}(H_{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{rig}})}

and then we conclude by Proposition
A.1.4
.
∎

Remark 6.5.5
.

It is possible to upgrade Corollary
6.5.4
to include Hopf algebra structures (with respect to the completed projective tensor product of locally convex
K
-vector spaces). Indeed, since
V\mapsto\underline{V}
